- **Change:** development, cumulative fd9009bb510a8351cb3f8f404f2db9d76c978134..830de9d075aeabe5461b7aca204acfb5fc1507ba; 357 frozen paths.
- **Reviewer:** separate independent security contexts in R1/R2 and explicitly attributed replacement R2 fix verification; coordinator consolidation, no human approval attributed.
- **Date:** first review2026-10-13; final committed-fix verification and canonical filing2026-10-15.
- **Separation from code review:** distinct dedicated security agents and empty checklists; actual peer reports exchanged only after preserved independent checkpoints. Precise reading limits and immutable-source reconciliation chronology follow below; no unseen-final or hours-long-gap claim.
- **Unsafe audit cross-reference:** no Rust/assembly/unsafe/audit-log implementation change; no unsafe re-audit or memory-safety proof.
- **Code-review cross-reference:** [paired code record](../code-reviews/2026-10-13-t056-parser.md).
- **Authority/evidence:** [ADR-0043](../../../decisions/0043-documentation-corpus-consistency.md), [approved plan](../../reports/2026-10-12-phase-b2-track2-preflight-and-plan.md), [original evidence](../../reports/2026-10-13-t056-parser-evidence/README.md).

## 1. Capability correctness

N/A for kernel operations/rights/transfers/revocation: this change introduces only host documentation tooling. The host command does not mutate repository settings or grant new authority. Both retained security rounds explicitly assess all six literal capability prompts.

## 2. Trust boundaries

OK for the applicable filesystem/input boundary: the public corpus is fixed, local-only analysis is not read, invalid UTF-8/nonregular/symlinked inputs and escaped or unsupported local destinations fail visibly. No network retrieval or Git requirement exists. The hostile-document checks exercise false-green hiding, exact target/line semantics, read canaries and error behavior. Kernel pointer/IPC prompts are N/A with their scoped justifications in the full reports.

## 3. Memory safety

N/A for new unsafe/raw-pointer/lifetime invariants: no runtime source change. OK for bounded host parsing safeguards and preserved original offsets within the documented grammar. Python memory safety and finite fixtures are not proof of acceptable resources for every input or Rust soundness.

## 4. Kernel-mode discipline

N/A for ISR/critical-section/kernel panic/allocation behavior. Host denial-of-service concerns were assessed separately: the confirmed mixed-delimiter quadratic-search regression is repaired and both opposite 32k directions pass recorded finite deadlines. All twelve closer classes, future eligible openers and partial consumption receive independent differential controls. The distinct master-plan allocation prompt is retained explicitly in each full security report.

## 5. Cryptography

N/A — no cryptographic primitive/protocol/key/randomness change. SHA-256 identifies evidence bytes and supplies no authenticity claim.

## 6. Secrets and logging

OK within scope: diagnostics and retained evidence contain public paths/source identities and fixture data, not credentials or imported private analysis. Source reading remains within the checked root/public ownership rules. No security-sensitive Debug or runtime logging implementation changes.

## 7. Dependencies

OK — Python 3.9+ standard library only; no package installation, Cargo dependency/toolchain/trust-policy mutation or kernel-TCB change. The primary syntax reference is separately attributed read-only research, not a runtime dependency.

## 8. Threat-model impact

OK — the checker enforces bounded mechanical documentation consistency and does not change the kernel threat model. Historical findings/measurements retain their source and limits; hosted/CI/task delivery and whole-phase gates remain outstanding. All 32 standard prompts, separate allocation item, P1–P12 and sixteen task criteria receive actual explicit outcomes in the retained reports.

## Verdict

**Approve** — both required review rounds and actual committed-fix verification complete for the selected Step 11 parser scope; zero unresolved verified source findings. Full T-056 delivery remains pending Step 12/13 and hosted evidence.

- **Change:** local development, cumulative fd9009bb510a8351cb3f8f404f2db9d76c978134..ca1f0735bed114228a4b8a165162e27fbacd67b3 (20 changed paths). Kickoff 9b62a06; T-049 quotation repair 0c62391; implementation ca1f073.

The paired contexts are independent; no invented elapsed-hours separation or code-review-first chronology is asserted. Actual peer cross-read, pair completion and canonical publication remain pending.
- **Unsafe audit cross-reference:** N/A — no Rust, assembly, unsafe annotation or audit-log delta. This is a host-tooling review, not renewed kernel/audit certification.

## Scope, authority and independent method

The [approved sequence](../../reports/2026-10-12-phase-b2-track2-preflight-and-plan.md), Accepted [ADR-0043](../../../decisions/0043-documentation-corpus-consistency.md), [T-056 contract](../../tasks/phase-b2/T-056-docs-consistency-automation.md), [parser brief](../../reports/2026-10-13-t056-parser-preflight.md) and [implementation account](../../reports/2026-10-13-t056-parser-implementation.md) define a deliberately partial Step 11. The delivered command checks links only; remaining index/ledger/fence/audit/live-state families and required existing-job wiring are Step 12. This is neither full T-056 delivery nor permission to move into C/D or publish past the PR49 barrier.

This security review is explicitly requested for the host parser's untrusted-input boundary. Kernel checklist wording is retained literally below, with direct applicability separated from analogous host risks. The attacker can control repository Markdown, names and symlinks; can exploit invalid UTF-8, misleading syntax and decoding, omitted inputs, or expensive parsing; cannot obtain network access or setting mutation from an implementation that never requests either. Source inspection is required in addition to tests. A green scan alone does not establish complete grammar or absence of defects.

Every evidence JSON was parsed in full; the 382 baseline hash records were individually compared with their Git objects at fd9009b, and every current public-Markdown/code hash in the implementation capture was compared with the committed tree. This includes machine-complete verification of repeated hash-map records, rather than pretending those records are 382 independently reread Markdown bodies. All retained raw capture files were read. Runtime Rust implementation was not reread in full: no executable/runtime diff exists, and the current security model and ADR corpus supply the relevant unchanged threat boundary. Existing Python fixture style was sampled in test-perf-harness.py, not claimed as a full unrelated-tool audit.

Initial oversized policy/archive reads produced truncation warnings. Missing ADR sections were reread in smaller file/range calls (including 0001–0009, 0021/0022, 0024/0026/0027/0028, 0037/0039/0040); archive ranges 30–160 and long lines 107–114 were recovered; the security master plan was then read separately in full. D2b tail and the B.2 index preamble/tail were reread after combined-output truncation. No finding depends on an omitted tool-output segment. Public historical prose was scanned by the checker; that is distinct from claiming a new substantive security audit of every historical report.

## Confirmed findings

### S01 — Medium — symlinked corpus roots bypass public-input isolation

**Evidence:** [source enumeration](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L564-L568) and [load](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L624-L634). `os.walk(start, followlinks=False)` still walks an initially supplied symlink directory. The code checks child directories but never checks `docs`, `.agents/skills`, or the `.agents` ancestor before enumeration. `load` rejects only a symlink at the leaf, not its parents. A synthetic `docs -> outside-directory` containing `x.md` was read and its deliberately missing destination appeared in diagnostics. A synthetic `.agents/skills -> docs/analysis/private` also read the otherwise excluded private `x.md` and printed the private canary destination. Both reports counted two Markdown inputs and emitted no unsupported-symlink-input finding.

**Impact:** a repository-controlled symlink causes host files outside the intended corpus/root, or explicitly local-only analysis, to be parsed; selected contents can leak through findings. This defeats the guide's explicit no-read/input-symlink boundary without any race. Only synthetic files were used to demonstrate it. Ordinary link resolution's escape/private checks do not protect the initial source-loading path.

**Action:** validate every enumeration root and all relevant ancestor components before walking; reject unsupported symlinked input roots/parents before opening files. Apply a shared canonical-root/local-only check at source load as defense in depth, covering direct, intermediate, dangling and loop aliases with actionable diagnostics. Add separate fixtures for docs, skills and `.agents` symlink ancestry, asserting that a mocked/canary reader is never invoked for forbidden source bytes. Preserve Git-free operation and explicit fixture-root semantics.

### S02 — Medium — list-relative closing fences silently hide real links

**Evidence:** [container/fence state](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L97-L109). The opening list marker's two-column content indentation is recorded but is not removed when matching subsequent closing fences. This valid list item closes its fence with two spaces of indentation inside the list, then contains an actual broken link:

~~~~text
- ```text
    ```
  [bad](missing.md)
~~~~

Independent result: `ok: true`, one file, **zero local targets**, zero findings. The four absolute spaces on line 2 exceed the regex's three-space allowance, even though list-relative indentation is two; line 3 remains wrongly masked as code. Conversely, a fenced block nested inside a list and blockquote is treated as prose and produces a false path finding; the exact three-line input is retained in the focused probe capture. The positive/negative error has one root cause: container state is not normalized consistently across lines.

**Impact:** adding ordinary supported list indentation can bypass the promised whole-corpus link gate. This is a source-controlled false green, not merely unsupported rendering polish.

**Action:** maintain container-relative indentation/quote state through opening, continuation and closing; extend fixtures to close fences at 0–3 spaces relative to list content, nested quote/list combinations and links immediately after closure. If a container form remains outside the bounded grammar, diagnose it explicitly rather than masking the remaining document. Do not repair the legitimate source text to satisfy the parser.

### S03 — Medium — HTML destinations are decoded twice and validate a different file

**Evidence:** [HTML link collection](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L328-L340) and [destination normalization](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L655-L666). HTMLParser already decodes attribute character references, then scan_links applies `unescape` again. With only a file named `a&b.md` present, this input passes:

```text
<a href="a&amp;amp;b.md">x</a>
<img src="a&amp;amp;b.md">
```

The actual HTML attribute denotes `a&amp;b.md`, which is absent. The checker decodes again to `a&b.md` and reports success. In the reverse fixture, creating only the actual `a&amp;b.md` target produces a false missing-target finding. Both href and src were independently reproduced.

**Impact:** the validator attests to the wrong destination; encoding can hide a broken actual target. The same source-kind confusion also risks applying Markdown backslash escapes to raw HTML attributes.

**Action:** preserve destination origin or normalize at collection exactly once. Markdown escape/entity decoding and HTML attribute decoding must follow their respective source rules before common URL/path processing. Add positive/negative tests with nested entity spellings, literal backslashes, UTF-8 percent fragments and both anchor/image attributes.

### S04 — Low — inline-code heading text is stripped as HTML/emphasis

**Evidence:** [heading inline text](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L285-L319). The function strips `<...>` before recognizing inline-code spans, then deletes backticks and applies underscore-emphasis stripping without preserving code-token context. Both valid heading fragments fail:

```text
# `<word>`
[x](#word)

# `_secret_`
[y](#_secret_)
```

Independent isolated probes return missing-anchor findings. Literal code content should survive heading text extraction; punctuation filtering removes the angle punctuation while retaining `word`, and the guide explicitly retains underscores. Existing `write_bytes` fixture misses leading/trailing underscores and HTML-looking code text.

**Impact:** valid navigation is rejected, encouraging damaging source edits and undermining the promised supported heading grammar. Incorrect slugs can also make wrong fragment spellings appear valid.

**Action:** tokenize heading inlines respecting code/HTML precedence, retain code text before stripping actual tags/emphasis, and test both correct and incorrect fragment spellings plus duplicate suffixes for these headings.

### S05 — Low — quoted-title parsing performs quadratic suffix copying

**Evidence:** [title_end](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L228-L239), especially line 237. Every ordinary title character slices `text[cursor:]` merely to test whether it begins with a blank-line pattern. A valid title with 100,000 / 200,000 / 400,000 / 800,000 `a` characters took 0.096 / 0.337 / 1.174 / 4.341 seconds, respectively, in separate independent Python 3.9 subprocesses. All completed successfully; no timeout or 16-MiB execution is claimed. The source establishes quadratic total copied characters, while the documented 16-MiB input bound permits much larger titles.

**Impact:** malicious repository text well inside the advertised size limit amplifies host CPU/memory-copy work and can occupy the future required CI step. This is host-tool availability, not a new kernel unbounded-loop defect.

**Action:** use indexed scanning or a compiled regex's position argument; only inspect a blank-line suffix when the current character is a newline. Review the other scanners for repeated full-tail slicing and add a bounded large-title regression without a brittle host-specific timing threshold. If an additional title/work limit is necessary, document and diagnose it explicitly.

## Checklist convention

The 32 prompts below are copied literally from the security standard and retained as inert tagged text. Each receives an independent disposition. N/A is only the direct kernel/crypto applicability judgment; it never waives the host-tool adversarial checks above. Item 33 is the distinct master-plan allocation/typed-error prompt.

## 1. Capability correctness

### Checklist 01 — N/A

```text
Every privileged operation introduced by this change requires a capability.
```

No kernel privileged operation or authority object is introduced. Host read authority is applicable separately and fails at source-root symlinks (S01).

### Checklist 02 — N/A

```text
The capability required is the **narrowest** one that permits the operation (no privilege escalation by accepting a broad capability where a narrow one would do).
```

No capability kind or right selection changes; the host command requests no credential or administrative authority.

### Checklist 03 — N/A

```text
Capabilities are checked **before** observable side effects. A failed check does not leak information that the call was attempted.
```

No capability-check ordering changes. Analogous validate-before-read discipline is flagged under S01; leaf checks are insufficient.

### Checklist 04 — N/A

```text
Capability transfer (if any) is move-only; no accidental cloning.
```

No capability value transfer or ownership implementation is touched; Python Link/Finding values are non-authority data.

### Checklist 05 — N/A

```text
Capability duplication (if intended) itself requires an explicit `Duplicate`-style authority.
```

No capability duplication API or token cloning is added; shared parser caches do not confer kernel rights.

### Checklist 06 — N/A

```text
Capability revocation (if applicable) takes effect atomically; no partial state where the revocation is half-applied.
```

No revocation state, concurrent kernel update or task capability table is modified.

## 2. Trust boundaries

### Checklist 07 — N/A

```text
Every input crossing from userspace to the kernel is validated before use.
```

No userspace-to-kernel entry changes. Host Markdown/filesystem input validation is applicable and flagged by S01–S04; invalid UTF-8/limits have positive rejection evidence.

### Checklist 08 — N/A

```text
Pointers from userspace are **never** dereferenced in kernel mode without going through a validated mapping.
```

No raw pointer dereference or kernel mapping change; host path traversal is separately checked, with S01 initial-source-read escape remaining.

### Checklist 09 — N/A

```text
Buffer lengths provided by userspace are range-checked against the actual mapped region.
```

No mapped user buffer lengths change. Host document reads have a 16-MiB byte bound; title parsing still amplifies permitted input (S05).

### Checklist 10 — N/A

```text
Message contents from userspace are parsed into typed structures; no raw bytes are consumed in privileged code.
```

No privileged message parser changes. Host links/findings use NamedTuple/dataclass structures, but destination origin is lost and decoding is wrong (S03).

### Checklist 11 — N/A

```text
Cross-task IPC does not grant the receiver any authority the sender did not have or did not explicitly transfer.
```

No IPC send/receive, transfer, rights attenuation or receiver authority changes.

## 3. Memory safety

### Checklist 12 — N/A

```text
Any new `unsafe` block meets [unsafe-policy.md](unsafe-policy.md).
```

No new or altered Rust unsafe block/function/impl/trait and no audit amendment required; the dependency is Python stdlib managed memory.

### Checklist 13 — N/A

```text
Invariants stated in `# Safety` sections hold for every call site, not only "the usual" one.
```

No Safety contract or unsafe caller changes. Existing kernel contracts remain outside this host delta; no blanket recertification is made.

### Checklist 14 — N/A

```text
No uninitialized memory is exposed. Where a buffer is declared but only partially filled, the unused portion is zeroed or the buffer is returned as `MaybeUninit`.
```

No partially initialized kernel or raw byte buffer is exposed; Python bytes/str values are initialized. S01 concerns unauthorized reads, not uninitialized memory.

### Checklist 15 — N/A

```text
No use-after-free. Lifetimes on raw pointers are reasoned about explicitly.
```

No raw pointers or manual lifetimes; managed parser objects have no observed use-after-free path in this code.

### Checklist 16 — N/A

```text
No aliasing violations. Mutable pointers to the same memory do not coexist.
```

No mutable raw aliases, shared kernel references or concurrency changes. Single-process mutable lists are ordinary Python objects.

## 4. Kernel-mode discipline

### Checklist 17 — N/A

```text
No allocation in interrupt service routines.
```

The command is host tooling and cannot run in a Tyrne interrupt handler; no ISR allocation path changes.

### Checklist 18 — N/A

```text
No unbounded loops in kernel mode; every loop has a documented termination.
```

No kernel loop changes. Host availability is applicable: S05 flags quadratic permitted-input work despite explicit byte/nesting bounds.

### Checklist 19 — N/A

```text
Critical sections are minimized. Scheduler state is held for as short a time as possible.
```

No scheduler lock, interrupt mask, critical section or shared kernel state changes.

### Checklist 20 — N/A

```text
No new kernel panic introduced on a hot path.
```

No kernel hot path or panic behavior changes. Host read/parse findings fail nonzero; this is not a guarantee against every host exception/exhaustion.

### Checklist 33 — N/A directly; host limit reviewed

```text
Every allocation path returns a typed error, not a panic, on exhaustion.
```

No kernel allocation path changes. Python allocation can raise MemoryError and no complete host-exhaustion recovery is claimed. Document-byte/nesting/reference-label checks produce structured findings before their explicit limits; S05 shows that bounded size alone does not provide a practical processing-work bound. This additional master-plan item is not silently merged with the 32 standard prompts.

## 5. Cryptography

### Checklist 21 — N/A

```text
No roll-your-own primitives. If a primitive is introduced, it is a well-known algorithm from a reviewed crate (or, if truly novel, it comes with a separate ADR and a cited proof or analysis).
```

No cryptographic primitive/protocol is added. SHA-256 evidence hashes identify bytes and do not authenticate source or secure boot.

### Checklist 22 — N/A

```text
Keys are never logged, returned in error messages, or exposed via `Debug`.
```

No cryptographic keys are handled. Host private-input disclosure through diagnostics is independently flagged as S01.

### Checklist 23 — N/A

```text
Constant-time comparisons are used where timing leaks matter.
```

No secret comparison or authentication decision depends on timing; path/string comparisons need not be constant time. S05 is resource amplification, not a key side channel.

### Checklist 24 — N/A

```text
Randomness comes from an acceptable source — not `rand::thread_rng` with no seeding story.
```

No randomness enters the delivered checker. Temporary-root random names come from stdlib fixture mechanics, not security protocol entropy.

### Checklist 25 — N/A

```text
Nonces, IVs, and salts are handled correctly per the primitive's contract.
```

No nonce, IV, salt or encryption contract exists in this delta.

## 6. Secrets and logging

### Checklist 26 — flagged

```text
Secrets (keys, tokens, capability bits) are not included in logs, panic messages, debug output, or error types.
```

S01 can read excluded local notes or out-of-root Markdown through corpus-root aliases and echo their destinations in diagnostics. Only synthetic canaries were read during this review.

### Checklist 27 — N/A

```text
`Debug` impls on security-sensitive types redact sensitive fields (or are not implemented at all).
```

No Rust Debug implementation or secret-bearing type changes. NamedTuple findings deliberately expose public diagnostic text; S01 must enforce that public-input premise.

Python interpreter/stdlib and trusted checker source are host tooling, not kernel-linked code. Repository Markdown is data, never executed. CLI has no Git, network, subprocess or setting-mutation dependency; test subprocesses run the local interpreter/CLI.

```text
`cargo-vet` trust decisions are updated accordingly.

Cargo dependency graph and supply-chain trust decisions are unchanged; audit/vet remain conditional on the first external crate, not falsely reported as CI results.

## 8. Threat-model impact

## Architectural principles P1–P12

| Principle | Disposition and adversarial reasoning |
|---|---|
| P1 — No ambient authority | N/A kernel; host read boundary flagged S01. No new syscall authority or administrative credential is requested. |
| P2 — Smallest defensible TCB | OK: parser stays offline host tooling; it adds no privileged kernel surface. Its correctness still matters to future CI assurance. |
| P3 — Userspace services | N/A runtime: no driver/filesystem/network placement changes. |
| P4 — Checks at trust boundaries | Flagged host analogy: S01 reads before proving permitted source identity; S02/S03 accept wrong or omitted navigation. No kernel capability boundary changes. |
| P5 — Rust-only kernel | OK: Python is host tooling explicitly authorized by ADR-0043; no Rust/assembly unsafe delta. |
| P6 — HAL separation | N/A: no HAL/BSP coupling changes. |
| P7 — No proprietary blobs | OK: text-only Python/docs/JSON/raw fixture evidence; no binary blob or external dependency introduced. |
| P8 — Recorded decisions | OK: Accepted ADR-0043 and approved Step 11/12 split authorize the tool. The three exact local-only analysis prefixes match existing ownership policy; no historical broken-link exception is added. |
| P9 — Repository English | OK: source/docs/report English; Unicode fixture data exercises matching and is not translated repository prose. |
| P10 — Mermaid diagrams | OK: no new diagram format introduced; report uses tagged literal syntax examples. |
| P11 — Reproducibility | Partial: independent same-source/cwd output equality and explicit interpreter/hash identity reproduced. Case-sensitive host checks are implemented. No hosted Linux or build-bit-reproducibility certification follows. S01 can import host-local state and must be fixed. |
| P12 — Declared incremental scope | OK for lifecycle honesty: parser-only partial delivery is explicit, all 16 T-056 criteria remain unchecked and status In Progress. Approval remains blocked on parser defects; Step 12 and final delivery cannot be inferred from a green corpus. |

## T-049 ownership and evidence preservation

The two D2b-007 reference examples are historical quotations, not active intended navigation. Explicit code delimiters and the new dated annotation preserve all old bytes after the recorded reversible transform. The adjacent immutable historical navigation and current ADR section reference remain active and checked. Original finding wording, severity, suggested fix, claims register and verdict have not been rewritten. Source D1 is unchanged; the author's false-positive repair lives in the parser/fixture. This is a bounded repair already owned by T-049's accepted contract, not a broken-link allowlist or archive-wide omission. Original baseline/output captures remain separate and unchanged.

Input exclusions were assessed independently: the three exact analysis prefixes predate this parser in .gitignore and identify non-repository material; links into them, including ordinary aliases, must fail. Source-root aliases are the confirmed S01 bypass. The code also prunes conventional `.git`, `.venv` and `__pycache__` directories; this is a narrower enumeration limitation beyond the guide's three analysis prefixes, and the independent synthetic `.venv` case was silently skipped. These are existing generated/local metadata ignore categories, so this pass does not call ordinary environment pruning an arbitrary historical-archive exemption; future tracked public Markdown under such names would need an explicit scope disposition rather than an assumed complete scan. This limitation is recorded, not hidden by the zero-finding count.

Exploratory cases involving raw HTML block rendering, quote-definition continuations and an inline comment before a fenced block were not promoted wholesale to defects: this is a bounded checker, unsupported relevant forms may be diagnosed, and block-first CommonMark precedence matters. S02 uses the direct supported list-fence counterexample; S03 uses exact HTML attribute semantics; S04 uses the guide's explicit code-text/underscore promise. No external renderer/network result is claimed.

### Tests-first and evidence-quality disposition

The preserved first regression output is 53 methods, 20 failures and two errors in 22.396 seconds against the original parser with tests-only changes. Its large-input method timed out on the title and therefore did not execute its later unmatched-backtick case; separate original peer/coordinator probes provide that case's before-behavior. The initial symlink canary comparison failed to normalize macOS path aliases. Before parser edits the author corrected it, added an actual `.agents/skills` canary and aliases to all three exact private prefixes; the distinct strengthened output records two methods and six failing subcases. The mixed list/fence fixture's old four-space code expectation was incorrect for a two-column list continuation. The retained correction uses six absolute spaces for code, keeps standalone four-space code, and adds the four-space actual-link control. The correction account cites CommonMark Example 270; my validation is source/fixture inspection and independent positive/negative probes, not a new network renderer result. Intermediate symlink-loop and escaped-HTML raw regressions are honestly labelled uncommitted correction-worktree observations without contemporaneous complete source hashes. ## R1 continuation checkpoint — approval held for newly disclosed attribute-context defect

**Current verdict supersedes the provisional 49a28a5 approval above: Changes requested / verification pending.** Before delivery of the prepared closure draft, the coordinator disclosed an additional source-bound defect at the same frozen 49a28a5. **R1-R10 — Medium — HTML attribute named-reference context can validate the wrong target.** The coordinator's `/tmp/tyrne-b22-step11-root-attribute-context-probe.json` (read completely) binds the result to parser SHA-256 `a18395194d6b1a6cb3671f8ea9c9723916089fc1bdea34d53162c039fb3922d0` at 49a28a5. HTMLParser's attribute handling applies text-context entity unescaping; the HTML attribute rule preserves a semicolon-less named prefix when the following character is ASCII alphanumeric or `=`. Thus these actual attribute destinations remain literal:

```text
<a href="a&notit;.md">
<img src="a&copyx.md">
```

The coordinator capture reports false success with only `a¬it;.md` and `a©x.md` decoys present, and rejects the actual literal target filenames in the reverse fixture. The provided primary rule is the [WHATWG named-character-reference state](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#named-character-reference-state); I have not fetched that page or independently executed this newly disclosed case at this checkpoint. The source-bound coordinator evidence is sufficient to hold approval, but is labelled distinctly from my earlier independent checks. A correction must preserve raw attribute spellings and implement attribute-context decoding rather than assuming HTMLParser's already-decoded values are semantically exact. Regression coverage should include href/src plus id/name, semicolon-present controls, alphanumeric/equals followers, and legitimate semicolon-less/numeric decoding. The no-double-decoding closure of S03 remains valid for its original demonstrated input, but is insufficient to certify all HTML attribute decoding.

Accordingly the latest host trust-boundary/threat-model axis status is **flagged pending R1-R10**; AC2 and the incremental portions of AC9/DoD1–3 remain pending this correction. All unchanged partial-versus-final lifecycle restrictions above remain in force. No final peer agreement or successful fix is asserted.

### S06 — Medium — numeric entity decoding silently deletes a scalar and validates a different destination

**Evidence:** [Markdown numeric decoding](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L341-L344) and [HTML numeric decoding](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L541-L546) both delegate to `html.unescape`. On the actual supported Python 3.9 host, that helper returns an empty string for `&#xFDD0;`, `&#xFFFF;` and `&#11;`. The WHATWG numeric-end algorithm emits the noncharacter scalar after identifying its parse error; it does not delete it. CommonMark's selected numeric grammar likewise does not authorize removing a valid noncharacter scalar. A bounded checker can implement the correct scalar semantics or explicitly diagnose an unsupported relevant value, but must not silently accept a different target.

With only ordinary `ab.md` present, each independent case below returns `ok: true`, one local target, no findings. The actual decoded target contains U+FDD0 and is not `ab.md`:

```text
<a href="a&#xFDD0;b.md">
<img src="a&#xFDD0;b.md">
[x](a&#xFDD0;b.md)
```

A filesystem-independent reproduction proves both false acceptance and false rejection for anchors:

```text
<span id="a&#xFDD0;b"></span>
[wrong](#ab)
```

This wrongly passes. Replacing the destination with `#a%EF%B7%90b` wrongly fails with a missing-fragment finding at line 2. The same two outcomes occur with `<a name="a&#xFDD0;b">`. Seven separately recorded scans establish three path false successes, two anchor false successes and two actual-anchor false rejections. The actual noncharacter filename case is **not** claimed on macOS, which rejected filename creation with EILSEQ; the decoy path still demonstrates wrong-target success, and the id/name controls avoid that host limitation entirely.

**Impact:** a repository-controlled numeric spelling can make the checker approve the wrong file/fragment. This defeats the declared source-specific decoding contract and future link gate, without network, race, privileged access or kernel exploit.

**Action:** replace generic `html.unescape` delegation for numeric values with source-appropriate scalar conversion or explicit supported-limit diagnostics. Preserve legitimate noncharacter/control values where required; handle null/surrogate/out-of-range replacement and HTML legacy numeric remappings deliberately. Add no-filesystem id/name actual/decoy regressions and path-decoy controls for both Markdown and HTML. Keep digit-length grammar and no-recursive-decoding controls; do not silently normalize away the problematic code point or modify documentation to hide the case. Recheck numeric performance with a bounded input/subprocess deadline if conversion admits long HTML digit strings.

### S06 closure and compatibility analysis

**S06 — Medium, closed at 6f9f6d5.** [The numeric decoder](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L351-L367) now retains valid Unicode scalars, replaces null/surrogate/out-of-range values with U+FFFD, and applies the legacy C1 table only to HTML. [Markdown's exact single-pass decoding](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L376-L379) retains its separate decimal/hex spelling limits and escape precedence; [HTML attribute decoding](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L570-L587) retains its source-context named-reference rules. This closes the original loss of FDD0/FFFF/control scalars without creating recursive entity decoding or broad normalization.

These semantics were checked against the primary rules actually read during this same R1 continuation history: valid noncharacters are parse errors but still emitted by the HTML numeric-end algorithm; null/surrogate/out-of-range values use replacement, and specified legacy C1 values map separately. [WHATWG numeric-end state](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#numeric-character-reference-end-state). CommonMark's complete-name/semicolon and decimal 1–7 / hex 1–6 spelling rules remain a different source grammar. [CommonMark 0.31.2 entities](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#entity-and-numeric-character-references). Named HTML prefixes without a semicolon remain literal before ASCII alphanumeric or equals in attributes. [WHATWG named-reference state](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#named-character-reference-state). These are the previously recorded actual primary fetches, not retroactive claims that the held 49a checkpoint fetched them.

The coordinator's four new tests-first methods on f548 report 25 failures, no errors; the long-token method passed on that 3.9 run, and the C1 method stopped at its first mismatch. The later Python 3.13 before-capture reports both 100,000-digit subcases failing **inside HTMLParser's implicit integer conversion before the callback**, with separate contemporaneous dirty-source hashes. I read those complete raw captures, rather than treating the intermediate 3.9 green as cross-version evidence.

[The HTML callback](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L625-L666) and [same-length ampersand masking](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L685) now prevent that implicit conversion. Original tag spelling is recovered using public tag position and raw-tag length, not a private parser override. The public raw-tag API is documented by [Python HTMLParser](https://docs.python.org/3.9/library/html.parser.html#html.parser.HTMLParser.get_starttag_text). Masking preserves newline/character positions, with actual source restored before attribute decoding. No process-wide integer limit is disabled. Significant digits are bounded before `int`, after leading zeros are removed; long HTML numeric spelling therefore does not require a giant integer conversion. This is a bounded conversion claim, not a formal global HTMLParser complexity proof.

My fresh rerun of all 100 previously executed attribute/S06 scans now matches every expected result. The original seven S06 mismatches are gone: all href/src/Markdown noncharacter decoys fail, id/name shortened fragments fail, and actual scalar fragments pass. The three prior **not-run** actual noncharacter filename cases remain explicitly not run because macOS rejects those names with EILSEQ; 103 records do not mean 103 executions.

An additional 344-check matrix validates decimal/hex scalar boundaries, controls, all 32 C1 values with an independently written expected table, invalid scalar replacement, exact Markdown numeric limits and escaped/nonrecursive spellings. End-to-end id/name actual and decoy fragments cover controls, C1, noncharacters, supplementary-plane values and replacement boundaries. Actual/decoy href/src/inline/reference paths cover filesystem-supported ©/é/U+0080/U+0081/U+007F names, with HTML 128 resolving to Euro and Markdown 128 remaining U+0080. Those successful actual filenames do not imply noncharacter-filename support. Unicode text, ampersands, CRLF, multiple tags/attributes, quoted `>`, uppercase names and duplicate-attribute diagnostics retain physical line positions after masking.

The first extended harness run retained five expectation mismatches: it expected one missing-path finding for each reference case, whereas this checker deliberately checks both definition and use, yielding two. I inspected that existing contract and changed only those five expectations in a distinct `-v2` harness; all 344 then pass. Both original harness/output and corrected harness/output are retained. No parser change or hidden failed-source result is represented by that harness correction.

Eight further subprocess cases within that matrix use 100,000 decimal/hex digits, both all-large digits and leading-zero spellings ending in ©, on **each actual interpreter**. Every case passes under a five-second deadline; the longest corrected run observed was about 0.058 seconds. The suite independently exercises its own two decimal long-token cases. This supports conversion/compatibility closure without inventing a Linux execution or an all-input work bound.

## Retained Historical Round 2 actual reconciliation security report

# Security review 2026-10-13 — T-056 parser, R2 INITIAL

- **Change:** development, cumulative `fd9009bb510a8351cb3f8f404f2db9d76c978134..6f9f6d5053aefae3528bba4410023e4830bcebcf`; 50 paths, 7,256 additions and 12 deletions. This INITIAL applies to the frozen latter SHA only.
- **Reviewer:** independent AI R2 security reviewer `/root/b22_step11_r2_security`, configured GPT-6 Astra/high with a fresh `fork:none` context. The maintainer is not represented as having approved this artifact.
- **Date:** 2026-10-13.
- **Separation from code review:** a distinct reviewer and empty security checklist after the existing code R1 pass; neither author nor R1/code R2 reviewer. No invented elapsed-hours separation. Same reviewer resumed its exact prerequisite/source checkpoints rather than replacing the review or waiving reads.
- **Unsafe audit cross-reference:** N/A; no Rust, assembly, runtime, `unsafe`, audit-log, dependency, workflow, required-policy or repository-settings delta.
- **Scope trigger:** the user explicitly requested this separate host-parser security axis. The parser turns repository-controlled bytes and filesystem names into a future gate's claims, so source authority, containment, fail-closed behavior and evidence integrity are relevant despite unchanged kernel security mechanisms.
- **Skills:** [perform-security-review](../../../../.agents/skills/perform-security-review/SKILL.md) and [conduct-review](../../../../.agents/skills/conduct-review/SKILL.md), using the [security master plan](master-plan.md). Root alone owns eventual canonical filing/index/trailers. No external review post is authorized or performed.
- **Independence boundary:** no current R2 peer initial/final and no actual R1 agent report has been read. The fully read author correction account and task history expose attributed prior findings, corrections and historical report identities; complete blinding is not claimed. The user-required both-INITIAL boundary takes precedence over the skill's usual prior-review reading order. Actual peer/R1 cross-reading and independent reconciliation remain for an appended continuation after root supplies them.

## 1. Capability correctness

**Axis outcome: OK for the applicable host authority boundary; literal kernel capability mechanisms are unchanged.** A malicious document may request names but cannot grant the checker network, Git, subprocess, repository-setting or write authority. The selected root and exact public corpus are the host authority boundary. Lexical traversal and resolved outside/private targets fail; source aliases fail before content opening. Target-ancestor directory metadata may be enumerated before final containment. The observed unread-canary checks establish content-read behavior, not a universal metadata-secrecy or TOCTOU guarantee. Explicit caller `--root` aliases/case are canonicalized; this is distinct from the documented exact-case check on link targets and source-prefix aliases.

The following S01–S32 identifiers are this review's sequential traceability ordinals. The standard itself uses unnumbered checkbox bullets; its eight-group order and every full literal prompt are preserved inside tagged text blocks. Each disposition includes an adversarial host analogue or a concrete reason for N/A.

```text
[S01] Every privileged operation introduced by this change requires a capability.
```

**OK —** No new Tyrne privileged operation exists. Host analogue: reads are restricted by the selected root/public corpus; the CLI execution audit observed no write, process or socket action.

```text
[S02] The capability required is the **narrowest** one that permits the operation (no privilege escalation by accepting a broad capability where a narrow one would do).
```

**OK —** No broad kernel capability is accepted. Host analogue: only five root files, docs Markdown and skills Markdown are enumerated; the three exact local-only prefixes are excluded, without configurable archive exemptions.

```text
[S03] Capabilities are checked **before** observable side effects. A failed check does not leak information that the call was attempted.
```

**OK —** No kernel check changes. Source-root/ancestor/leaf aliases and nonregular content reads fail before opening; outside/private canaries were never opened. Resolved target containment is enforced before content reads, with the metadata/TOCTOU limitation stated above.

```text
[S04] Capability transfer (if any) is move-only; no accidental cloning.
```

**N/A —** No capability or IPC transfer is added. Python Link/Finding values copy source metadata, which conveys no kernel authority and causes no external action.

```text
[S05] Capability duplication (if intended) itself requires an explicit `Duplicate`-style authority.
```

**N/A —** No capability duplication operation is introduced; repeated references only repeat checks and do not mint authority.

```text
[S06] Capability revocation (if applicable) takes effect atomically; no partial state where the revocation is half-applied.
```

**N/A —** No revocation state or concurrent kernel mutation changes. The read-only host tool does not promise a race-free filesystem snapshot.

## 2. Trust boundaries

**Axis outcome: flagged — SF01 and SF02 block Step 11 security approval.** Host input validation must preserve what is being checked, not merely keep the interpreter alive. Source-specific entity decoding is explicit and has useful existing regressions, yet URL splitting can silently change the target. Physical-line consumers also disagree. Strong positive/negative controls demonstrated both defects; the existing green corpus and 62 methods do not cover them.

```text
[S07] Every input crossing from userspace to the kernel is validated before use.
```

**flagged —** The literal userspace/kernel boundary is unchanged; the applicable untrusted-document-to-check-result analogue is defective: SF01 selects a normalized decoy and SF02 manufactures valid fragments.

```text
[S08] Pointers from userspace are **never** dereferenced in kernel mode without going through a validated mapping.
```

**OK —** No kernel pointer is introduced. Host analogue: filesystem path containment and source aliases are checked before content opening; traversal, outside aliases and all three private-prefix canaries were rejected unread.

```text
[S09] Buffer lengths provided by userspace are range-checked against the actual mapped region.
```

**OK —** No user mapping is involved. Host inputs are bounded at 16 MiB+1 read and rejected above 16 MiB; depth 65 and a1,000-character reference-definition label fail visibly. These are local bounds, not a global memory guarantee.

```text
[S10] Message contents from userspace are parsed into typed structures; no raw bytes are consumed in privileged code.
```

**flagged —** No privileged raw IPC parsing changes. Link/Finding structures retain source origin, but SF01 subsequently loses origin-specific characters through urlsplit and SF02 uses incompatible physical-line models.

```text
[S11] Cross-task IPC does not grant the receiver any authority the sender did not have or did not explicitly transfer.
```

**N/A —** No task or IPC authority changes. Host analogue: external schemes are skipped, never fetched; a document cannot cause subprocess/Git execution or a settings write.

## 3. Memory safety

**Axis outcome: OK within the host-language scope; literal unsafe obligations are N/A.** Source/target reads validate containment, regular-file type, size and UTF-8. FIFO source and source-line target probes returned immediately with findings; a link that only checks a FIFO's existence does not open it and is not a blocked-read defect. No kernel/runtime/FFI code changes, so Rust Miri, QEMU and unsafe-log reconciliation would not test this delta. This does not certify CPython memory safety.

```text
[S12] Any new `unsafe` block meets [unsafe-policy.md](unsafe-policy.md).
```

**N/A —** No unsafe block or audit annotation changes in the cumulative diff; no new unsafe sign-off can be inferred from Python tests.

```text
[S13] Invariants stated in `# Safety` sections hold for every call site, not only "the usual" one.
```

**N/A —** No Rust # Safety contract changes. The reviewed host preconditions are checked through read_utf8, source alias checks and typed path/parser findings; semantic exceptions SF01/SF02 remain flagged.

```text
[S14] No uninitialized memory is exposed. Where a buffer is declared but only partially filled, the unused portion is zeroed or the buffer is returned as `MaybeUninit`.
```

**OK —** No native uninitialized buffer is created. Byte reads are length-bounded, UTF-8 decoded, and invalid UTF-8 source fails with the observed physical line 2; unread private/outside canaries never enter diagnostics.

```text
[S15] No use-after-free. Lifetimes on raw pointers are reasoned about explicitly.
```

**N/A —** No raw pointer or lifetime-bearing runtime code is changed; the host code uses Python-managed values and no FFI. This is not a proof about the interpreter implementation.

```text
[S16] No aliasing violations. Mutable pointers to the same memory do not coexist.
```

**N/A —** No mutable raw-pointer alias is added. Filesystem aliases are a separate host issue and were tested explicitly, rather than conflated with Rust aliasing.

## 4. Kernel-mode discipline

**Axis outcome: OK for the bounded host work examined; literal kernel discipline is unchanged.** Every one of 292 module-level probe processes and 8 minimal direct CLI reproductions completed under its deadline; none timed out or crashed. Ordinary negative findings are expected outcomes, not 292 passing assertions. Large-case results support the stated local bounds only. Python 3.13 rejects very long source-line integers via its integer-string limit, while3.9 converts then rejects bounds; both fail visibly, though diagnostics differ.

```text
[S17] No allocation in interrupt service routines.
```

**N/A —** No ISR changes. Host allocation occurs in the Python process; bounded-read/nesting and adversarial-size probes address its applicable resource exposure.

```text
[S18] No unbounded loops in kernel mode; every loop has a documented termination.
```

**OK —** No kernel loop changes. Host scanners advance bounded offsets/indexes; 2M-character title,1000 distinct backtick runs,1M-digit HTML numeric and100k-digit line-number cases terminated under15-second subprocess deadlines. No global linear-time or memory-exhaustion guarantee is claimed.

```text
[S19] Critical sections are minimized. Scheduler state is held for as short a time as possible.
```

**N/A —** No scheduler lock or critical section changes. The host tool is sequential; the review harness concurrency is external to delivered code.

```text
[S20] No new kernel panic introduced on a hot path.
```

**OK —** No kernel panic path is added. Malformed syntax, UTF-8, absent roots, loops, size/depth bounds and special-file reads produced findings/nonzero CLI status in executed cases, without an uncaught exception. MemoryError/global exhaustion remains outside the promised contract.

The master plan adds a distinct33rd allocation prompt; it is not silently counted among the standard's32:

```text
[M33] Every allocation path returns a typed error, not a panic, on exhaustion.
```

**N/A for kernel allocation —** no allocator/kernel path changes. The host analogue is bounded source reads/nesting and typed parser/path diagnostics, which were exercised. Python allocation exhaustion can still raise MemoryError or encounter OS termination; the guide makes no global exhaustion-recovery guarantee. No process-wide integer limit is changed. Demanding a formally total host allocator would exceed this declared bounded checker scope.

## 5. Cryptography

**Axis outcome: N/A for delivered cryptography; evidence hashing is appropriate standard-library use.** No algorithm, key, protocol or entropy boundary is changed.

[S21] No roll-your-own primitives. If a primitive is introduced, it is a well-known algorithm from a reviewed crate (or, if truly novel, it comes with a separate ADR and a cited proof or analysis).
```

**N/A —** No cryptographic primitive or protocol is delivered. SHA-256 used to bind review evidence is the existing standard hashlib primitive, not a new kernel crypto implementation.

```

**N/A —** No key-bearing object or crypto error channel is introduced. Host logs contain public-source locations/errors; private/outside canaries were not read or printed.

[S23] Constant-time comparisons are used where timing leaks matter.
```

**N/A —** No secret-dependent authentication or comparison exists. Equality of public paths, anchors and recorded hashes needs no constant-time operation.

[S24] Randomness comes from an acceptable source — not `rand::thread_rng` with no seeding story.
```

**N/A —** The parser uses no randomness. Temporary fixture naming is review/test harness isolation, not a security token or kernel RNG.

```text
[S25] Nonces, IVs, and salts are handled correctly per the primitive's contract.
```

**N/A —** No nonce, IV, salt, cipher or replay state is introduced.

## 6. Secrets and logging

**Axis outcome: OK for applicable host diagnostics.** The checker does not need credentials and never fetched an external destination. Guards rejected any attempted content open of reviewer-created private/outside canaries; no guard fired. This says nothing about filesystem races or filenames observed during directory enumeration. Real local-only private files were not read by the reviewer.

```text
[S26] Secrets (keys, tokens, capability bits) are not included in logs, panic messages, debug output, or error types.
```

**OK —** No credential/token input, environment-secret dump or private-file content channel is added. Actual canary reads were guarded and absent; public destination text can appear in diagnostics, so no confidentiality promise is made for secrets embedded in public Markdown itself.

```text
[S27] `Debug` impls on security-sensitive types redact sensitive fields (or are not implemented at all).
```

**N/A —** No Rust Debug implementation or security-sensitive type is changed. JSON exposes only documented report metadata and findings, not internal interpreter objects or secret state.

**Axis outcome: flagged — SF01 concerns stdlib behavior, not an added dependency.** Offline/no-package/no-Git execution was independently reproduced. Trusting a high-level standard-library function does not remove the need to inspect its normalization behavior across the advertised Python floor/current interpreter.

```text
[S28] Any new dependency has been evaluated under [infrastructure.md](infrastructure.md) dependency-addition rules.
```

**OK —** No third-party dependency, installation or lockfile change is introduced. Imports in all three delivered Python files were inspected; the executed CLI worked with empty PATH.

```

**flagged —** The dependency boundary is host Python stdlib, not kernel-linked code. HTMLParser implicit conversion was addressed by the author, but SF01 demonstrates another version-sensitive stdlib transformation in urllib.parse.urlsplit; source context must own that behavior.

```

**N/A —** No external Rust dependency/trust decision changes; cargo-vet/cargo-audit remain planned conditional gates, not checks claimed by this review.

## 8. Threat-model impact

**Axis outcome: OK for scope reconciliation, with host integrity blockers carried explicitly.** No changes to capability/syscall/IPC/boot/MMU/scheduler mechanisms, ADRs, unsafe audit or protection settings are present. A false-green host checker can mislead a future documentation gate, which justifies Medium severity and correction before Step 11 acceptance; it is not evidence of kernel authority escalation. HIGH T-038, Tracks C/D and the PR49 publication barrier remain open, unaffected.

```

**OK —** The unchanged security model describes a pre-alpha capability microkernel. The added surface is host evidence integrity; SF01/SF02 block this step without implying a new kernel exploit or reopening settled architectural scope.

```

**OK —** No kernel threat-model shift requires an ADR/update. Bounded host input and offline/read-only constraints fit Accepted ADR-0043; fixes belong to this parser contract, not a weakened exception or a phase-closure claim.

## Confirmed findings and required corrections

### R2-SF01 — Medium — implicit URL normalization changes supported targets and differs by Python version

**Location:** [tools/docs_consistency.py:1032](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1032), after origin-specific decoding at 1026; supported behavior is described in [guide lines 135–151](../../../guides/ci.md#L135).

**Adversary/control:** create only `foo.txt` and a root `README.md` containing `[x](< foo.txt>)` (there is one literal leading space inside the angle destination). Run `python -B tools/check-docs.py --root <fixture> --json`. On actual Python 3.9.6 it exits 1 and reports missing ` foo.txt`; on actual Python 3.13.14 it exits 0, accepting `foo.txt`. Reverse the fixture so only ` foo.txt` exists:3.9 exits 0 and3.13 exits 1 looking for `foo.txt`. Both runs select one local target. This is not an absent-link or skipped-test artifact. The same version split was observed for leading numeric references `&#32;`, `&#1;`, `&#31;`. Percent-encoded `%20foo.txt` correctly preserves the leading space on both interpreters.

**Cause and contract:** `urlsplit(value)` strips leading C0/space on newer Python, while the guide promises source-specific decoding exactly once followed by UTF-8 URL decoding, preservation of valid numeric scalars, angle destinations and Python 3.9+. Python documents the normalization changes; CommonMark permits spaces inside angle destinations and treats numeric references as characters. CommonMark also explicitly leaves some renderer URL normalization choices open, so this finding does **not** claim universal renderer parity. It is the demonstrated inconsistent supported-source contract and target identity, with an actual/decoy false green, that blocks acceptance. [Python URL parsing documentation](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/urllib.parse.html#urllib.parse.urlsplit), [CommonMark links](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#links).

**Context distinction:** WHATWG browser URL parsing deliberately removes ASCII tabs/newlines and trims leading/trailing C0/space. Therefore the HTML href/src tab/newline probes are **not** reported as a character-reference decoder defect. Both Python versions drop those decoded characters in the current implementation; raw HTML leading-space behavior nevertheless differs between3.9 and3.13. Markdown entity/inline/reference/image probes showed the same suffix-joining behavior, which needs an explicit source-context policy. The stronger blocker above needs no assumption that every renderer must preserve raw control characters. [WHATWG basic URL parser](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-basic-url-parser).

**Impact:** a repository author can obtain an exit0 for a different local file, and advertised compatible interpreters disagree on whether the same source passes. Scope is host gate integrity, not code execution or an observed leak.

**Action:** make target splitting/normalization explicit and origin-aware before calling normalization-prone helpers. Preserve the declared Markdown target identity, or diagnose a deliberately unsupported ambiguous spelling visibly; define HTML URL normalization separately and consistently. Add actual-versus-decoy tests for leading spaces/C0, decoded tabs/newlines, inline/reference/image and HTML cases on both supported interpreters. Preserve percent encoding and source line diagnostics; rerun the corpus. Do not hide the issue by excluding source or changing process-wide settings.

### R2-SF02 — Medium — Unicode splitting creates nonexistent headings/source lines; CR diagnostics disagree

**Locations:** [tools/docs_consistency.py:862](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L862) for heading extraction and [1067](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1067) for source-line bounds; related block/inline splitting at 145,212,385,748 and LF-only diagnostic indexes at 643/672 need the same line model.

**Minimal source/control:** write `README.md` with the Python string `"# one\u2028# phantom\n\n[x](#phantom)\n"`, i.e. an actual U+2028 scalar between the two hash-marked words. Both actual Python 3.9.6 and3.13.14 CLIs exit0 with one fragment. CommonMark recognizes only LF, CR and CRLF as line endings, so `# phantom` is not a new ATX heading and cannot supply that automatic fragment. Replacing U+2028 with LF/CR/CRLF is a real-heading positive control and passes. Independently, a source target containing only `"a\u2028b"` is accepted for `#L2` although it has one physical source line under that line model. The same phantom behavior was executed for VT, FF, FS, GS, RS, NEL and U+2029. [CommonMark characters and lines](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#characters-and-lines).

**Cause:** Python `str.splitlines()` recognizes a wider set of boundaries than Markdown/source physical lines; heading and source-line bounds inherit that behavior. Meanwhile `Markdown.newlines` and HTML raw-attribute offsets count only LF. The independent CR-only source `"before\r\r[x](missing)"` therefore reports line 1 rather than physical line 3; CRLF attribute-line control correctly reports line 4. These are related source-line consistency failures, not new rendered syntax.

**Impact:** adversarial text can manufacture a validated nonexistent fragment/line and make diagnostic location inaccurate. The source checker explicitly supports ATX/setext and actual source-line bounds; a bounded grammar may reject unsupported input but must not silently certify a phantom anchor.

**Action:** centralize the intended LF/CR/CRLF physical-line iteration and offset indexing while preserving original byte/text positions. Apply it consistently to block masks, inline/reference continuation, headings, HTML offsets and `#L` counts. Add non-line Unicode/control actual-versus-phantom negatives, real LF/CR/CRLF positives and CR-only physical-line assertions. Fix parser behavior; no archive exclusion or historical-text mutation is justified.

## Architectural principles P1–P12

Each principle was read in full before source inspection. Literal kernel applicability and host analogues are separated below; no exception to a principle is proposed.

| Principle | Individual disposition |
|---|---|
| P1 — No ambient authority | OK: no kernel authority added; host read root/public-prefix boundaries enforced in executed containment/canary probes, no network/process/write authority consumed. |
| P2 — Smallest defensible trusted computing base | OK: parser remains offline host tooling outside kernel TCB; stdlib surface is inspected and SF01 flagged. |
| P3 — Drivers, filesystems, and network stacks live in userspace | N/A to new driver/service placement; no such runtime subsystem changes. Host filesystem reads do not move a filesystem into the kernel. |
| P4 — Capability checks at every trust boundary | Kernel unchanged; host analogue flagged for result integrity by SF01/SF02, while read-containment checks pass. |
| P5 — Rust-only for kernel code | OK: no non-Rust kernel/HAL/service code; Python is explicitly approved host tooling under ADR-0043. |
| P6 — HAL separation | N/A to this delta; no register, CPU, board or HAL dependency changes. |
| P7 — No proprietary binary blobs in the kernel | OK: no binary dependency/blob/build artifact introduced; text evidence remains auditable. |
| P8 — Decisions are recorded as ADRs | OK: Accepted ADR-0043 and approved Step 11/12 plan govern this implementation; no new architectural exception/ADR status change. |
| P9 — English in the repository, Turkish in chat | OK: changed source, author explanation, criteria and this report are English; quoted historical bytes preserved. |
| P10 — Mermaid for diagrams | N/A: no architectural diagram added; no binary diagram introduced. |
| P11 — Reproducibility from the toolchain up | Kernel-build property unchanged. Exact source/output hashes reproduced on the actual installed interpreters; SF01 flags host portability across advertised versions, not a claimed same-toolchain binary-reproducibility violation. No cargo-vet/audit or hosted/binary comparison is invented. |
| P12 — No half-finished subsystems on main | OK at development boundary only: Step 11/12 split is explicit, T056 remains In Progress with 16 unchecked criteria, no merge/publication or whole-task acceptance. Findings must be corrected before this step is accepted. |

### Evidence identity validation

- All 2,311 public-Markdown hash entries were compared individually: baseline 382→`fd9009b`; implementation 385→`ca1f073`; correction 386→`49a28a5`; attribute 386→`f548798`; final compatibility 386→`6f9f6d5`. Intermediate numeric 386 matched 384 final blobs; the other two documents were reconstructed by removing only their final compatibility paragraph, yielding both exact recorded intermediate hashes. No historical capture was treated as proof of the final code.
- All 71 declared old-capture comparisons passed, including both 24-entry arrays against actual `f548798` Git blobs and current capture bytes. All 48 additional code/capture/policy/T049 checks passed. The intermediate numeric parser was reconstructed by reversing only the final raw-tag recovery/ampersand-mask change and matched `fe646f459b6ae31591304ec73d479bece79dd4e08b3d7e886a0ce66764572413` exactly.
- T049's three delimiter transformations and dated annotation were reversed; the result exactly matched its recorded `9b62a064` source blob/hash. No other historical finding, severity or verdict byte was changed. This is a bounded source clarification under its owner, not an archive exclusion. Older parser false-positive dispositions remain distinct from actual source defects.
- Final parser SHA `f1987b11cbec50b02141e19213cdd1761e8d6a30cea62ae688692c20c26ed79f`; CLI `9f4e184a8752e60d46c201135bb2e38e21171fb4be16e21f56796a3f056dfa1b`; tests `7dcf9a80b685fa65f1e5426a612919c43d8ea0ee432e260d6b0013b76143ad5b`.
- All 43 added-file patch reconstructions matched consumed bytes. Full cumulative diff SHA-256: `0cca6efefaef27138de96b872ac7d00b7f198d6d378275b970f1212f0b1796e5`.

### Independent execution and adversarial controls

All executions were local macOS 27.0.1 arm64, with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `-B` and an explicit reviewer-owned TMPDIR. No installation, branch, worktree, repository write, remote operation, private-file content read or subagent was used. Every subprocess had a 10/15/30/45-second deadline as recorded; no blocking wait exceeded the review communication bound.

| Actual command/control | Result |
|---|---|
| In-memory compile of all 3 Python files; `ast.parse(..., feature_version=(3,9))` | Pass; no invented formatter/linter. |
| Xcode Python 3.9.6 `-B tools/test-check-docs.py` |62 methods, exit0,1.562s unittest time. |
| Homebrew Python 3.13.14 same suite |62 methods, exit0,1.403s unittest time. |
| Each interpreter, absolute CLI `--json`, both repository cwd and reviewer tmp cwd, `PATH=''` | All 4 exit0,386 files/6650 local targets/772 fragments/no findings; exact stdout SHA `0facc74c6b5462369d494845f30d611d91a7c02e83d37e83eab1ab05159b2e7b`. |
| Independent Git-tracked public inventory and content hashes |386 exact members/hash values equal the final capture; clean tree contains no additional tracked/public source discrepancy. Git is used by reviewer validation, never by delivered checker. |
| Actual CLI run under Python audit hook denying write-open/process/socket events | Exit0, same corpus JSON, `AUDIT []`; no forbidden event attempted in that run. |
| Negative CLI text/JSON and repeated JSON | Exit1; sorted file/line findings; byte-identical repeated JSON. Unsupported `--checks all` exits 2; missing root exits 1 with input finding. |
|146 independent fixture specifications, each under both interpreters |292 module-level subprocess executions,0 crashes/timeouts. Each record preserves observed report, guarded opens and duration; these are observations, not 292 asserted passes. |
| Four minimal SF01/SF02 roots through real CLI under both interpreters |8 direct CLI reproductions; SF01 actual/decoy version reversal and SF02 false greens confirmed. |

The independent fixtures cover all three exact private prefixes and aliases with unread canaries; encoded traversal/outside leaf/ancestor aliases; source roots/ancestors/leaves/dangling/loops; source/line-read FIFOs; exact linked-name case; public archive/build/hidden/private-ish/skills positives and noncorpus root-file control; source/target invalid UTF-8; percent validation; entity/escape/URL actual-decoy controls; headings/HTML anchors/CRLF and CR offsets; malformed/unclosed syntax and relevant duplicate attributes; code/comment/title/escaped-HTML precedence; visible size/depth/label-limit failures;2M-character title,1000 distinct backtick runs,100k/1M-digit HTML numerics and6000/100k-digit source-line integers. The measured bounded large cases terminated, with no formal global resource bound inferred.

### Harness corrections, disposed hypotheses and limitations

The first evidence-validation script attempt failed before writing its result because an eagerly evaluated default treated string `source_identity` as a dict. It was corrected and the entire validation rerun. The initial C1 HTML anchor positive used U+0080 rather than the required legacy U+20AC; its rejection was correct, not a finding. A corrected U+20AC positive was actually executed and passed both interpreters. An extensionless `docs/loop` is not source Markdown; actual `docs/loop.md` and source-prefix loops were separately executed and diagnosed. The initial case-variant root test selected an empty directory; the populated followup confirmed canonicalization of caller-selected roots. No root-case finding is asserted. No case was silently skipped or counted as executed after a host restriction.

Raw HTML URL tab/newline removal is specified browser behavior and is not SF01 by itself. A FIFO checked only for existence is not opened, so that control does not demonstrate a hang. Outside/private target ancestor metadata can be enumerated before final containment, but no protected content was opened; no universal metadata noninterference or TOCTOU guarantee is asserted. No macOS noncharacter filename creation was needed: noncharacter anchor controls executed successfully. CR-only offset error and Unicode phantom boundaries are retained under SF02, not confused with renderer differences.

This review does not claim complete CommonMark rendering, global resource/MemoryError recovery, Rust soundness, kernel/QEMU execution, hosted Linux, six live remote check attestations, maintainer approval, T056 delivery, Track B.2 closure or publication authority. All 16 T056 criteria remain unchecked and no date_done exists. Step 12 owns remaining check families/required existing-job enforcement/actual hosted evidence. HIGH T-038, Tracks C/D and PR49 publication barrier remain.

### Independent reconciliation and syntax authority

**R2-C01 — agree, remains a blocker pending correction verification.** The code reviewer labels this Major under the code standard. Under this review's host-security assessment its demonstrated effect is Medium result-integrity failure: multiline HTML metadata can manufacture heading anchors/suffixes, create false reference diagnostics, and suppress genuine later links through fence state. There is no demonstrated kernel exploit or new Critical/High finding. These classifications use different review axes; neither grants approval.

Frozen locations are [parser](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py) lines 136–190 (block-state decisions), 676–685 (pass order), 704 onward (definitions), 813–817 (HTML tag cursor skip without earlier metadata isolation), and 860–884 (heading/suffix extraction). The explicit [contract](../../../guides/ci.md) line 147 says attributes cannot create comments, links or anchors and unrelated prose remains checked. CommonMark allows newlines in quoted attribute values; a `div` starts a type-6 HTML block that ends at a blank line, so the demonstrated following Markdown is outside that metadata block. These specific semantics were actually checked in [CommonMark raw HTML](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#raw-html) and [HTML blocks](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#html-blocks), including the multiline-attribute examples. This establishes the supplied cases without demanding a complete renderer.

All six coordinator attribute cases were rerun on both interpreters: five mismatches and one real-heading control per interpreter. The additional single-quoted ghost and reference-use variants reproduce the defect. An additional fence-transition variant is stronger than checking exit alone: with a metadata backtick on line 2, a real broken link on line 5, then a real fenced literal on line 8, the old parser reports only the **literal line-8 `also-missing.md`** and misses the real line-5 `missing.md`. A real HTML `id` alongside multiline metadata remains a passing control. Required repair: isolate attribute metadata before it can influence block/comment/definition/heading state, retain actual relevant HTML attributes and physical offsets, and preserve ordinary prose/code controls.

**ROOT-R2-TABLE01 — independently agree after coordinator disclosure, Medium.** This was not an INITIAL discovery by this reviewer. Frozen `Markdown.__init__` lines 676–685 runs shared comment/inline/anchor passes without table cell/row isolation; code-span and label scans consequently cross cell boundaries. GFM parses each table cell's inlines independently, treats escaped pipes as cell content, requires matching header/delimiter cell counts, and ignores surplus body cells. Those rules and Examples 200, 203 and 204 were actually read in the [GFM table extension](https://github.github.com/gfm/#tables-extension-).

The coordinator's ten table cases were independently rerun on both interpreters. Cross-cell and cross-row backticks hide real links; a comment spanning cells also hides a real link; an unescaped pipe inside apparent HTML `id` manufactures a nonexistent anchor; a label spanning cells fabricates a path check. Five supplied controls behave correctly. Reviewer-designed controls preserve same-cell comments, actual HTML ids, ordinary pipe labels, same-cell labels and escaped-pipe labels. Companion mismatches show an escaped pipe in a table HTML id rejected as a missing anchor and an ignored surplus body cell checked as a real link. These are the same missing table-context family, not additional severity-inflating findings.

The bounded-checker contract permits explicit relevant unsupported-syntax findings; it does not justify silent green for hidden links/nonexistent anchors or manufacturing a path from two cells. Cross-cell label and surplus-cell observations are false-positive controls under the table semantics, not independent proof that all renderer features must be implemented. A repair may implement bounded isolation or clearly diagnose unsupported relevant syntax while retaining the supported ordinary/same-cell controls. It must not exclude tables or historical archives wholesale.
