- **R2-SF01, Medium, closed.** [Origin-specific navigation decoding](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L530) and the explicit delimiter handling in [scan_links](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1228) avoid `urlsplit`'s implicit target substitution. Markdown preserves the supported decoded leading C0/space and tab/CR/LF identity; raw HTML applies its separate declared URL cleanup before percent decoding. Actual-versus-decoy controls, inline/image/reference cases, percent-encoded spaces and composed table/HTML cases pass on both actual interpreters. HTML's specified cleanup is still not misreported as an entity-decoder bug. There is no new universal renderer-parity claim.
- **R2-SF02, Medium, closed.** [physical_lines](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L96), block/heading iteration, HTML physical offsets and [source-line bounds](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1309) use LF/CR/CRLF consistently. Eight non-line Unicode/control separators no longer create phantom headings or source lines. CR-only missing-target diagnostics report physical line 3; LF/CR/CRLF positives and invalid-UTF-8 offsets pass. Composed CR table and later multiline-HTML diagnostics retain lines 3 and 9 respectively.
- **R2-C01, CODE Major / host-security Medium, closed.** [blocks](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L238) shields complete prose HTML tag metadata from block construction; [Markdown](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L881) keeps definition/heading passes separate from real raw attributes. Full path/line/code/message assertions now require the real missing link on line 5, not the old substituted literal on line 8. Ghost/suffix/reference metadata, both fence characters, real IDs/hrefs, escaped tags and actual code controls pass. The withdrawn ambiguous title fixture and the five path/line-oracle mistakes remain historical fixture QA, not additional source findings.
- **ROOT-R2-TABLE01, coordinator Medium, closed.** [table_cells](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L158), [opening_fence/table_interrupt](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L196) and the [per-cell Markdown pass](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L910) isolate rendered cells/rows before inline code, comments, HTML and reference parsing. Escaped pipes are processed before per-cell HTML/reference interpretation. Original raw cell ranges and header width remain recorded; surplus body navigation is omitted under GFM semantics. Cross-cell comments/backticks/labels, fabricated IDs, ordinary pipe controls, same-cell code/comments, escaped-pipe IDs, short/surplus rows, global references, following real code blocks and long backtick controls pass. No source corpus is excluded to obtain that result.

These closures follow the primary rules actually read during this review: [CommonMark lines](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#characters-and-lines), [CommonMark HTML blocks](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#html-blocks), [GFM tables](https://github.github.com/gfm/#tables-extension-), [Python URL parsing](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/urllib.parse.html#urllib.parse.urlsplit) and [WHATWG URL input cleanup](https://url.spec.whatwg.org/#concept-basic-url-parser). The author's additional cmark implementation citations are attributed author evidence; this reviewer does not claim to have executed or independently read an external renderer from those citations.

## Actual completed reading and execution

All prerequisite reads and their proper ordered ADR chronology remain valid and unchanged. After receiving the complete freeze, I again read the **full current tests first** (1–180, 181–360, 361–550, 551–734), then the full current module (1–225, 226–450, 451–675, 676–900, 901–1120, 1121–1323), CLI 1–41, correction account, guide, full task, evidence README, dependency-order modified diffs and all new evidence. Each nonrepetitive JSON field and raw capture was fully consumed. All nine outstanding long captures were completed, including every failure traceback/subcase. Repeated hash maps/preservation arrays were checked entry by entry by machine, not represented as prose full reads.

The final cumulative binding accounts for all 90 paths: 44 unchanged INITIAL full-read artifacts carry forward by exact hash, and 46 current full-read/bound artifacts cover the correction set plus the unchanged reread CLI. Added-file cumulative patches reconstruct byte-for-byte to their consumed bodies; modified patches were read in dependency order. The original 115 ledger read entries and the entire correction checkpoint remain exact. No pending source/evidence truncation remains. A convenience reread of old report/source excerpts exceeded one outer output cap; the missing old report lines 220–245 were immediately recovered. One guessed evidence directory did not exist; its failed lookup was corrected before reading and is not counted as coverage.

Independent commands were orchestrated by retained reviewer scripts with actual `/usr/bin/python3` 3.9.6 and `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.13` 3.13.14. All fixture/probe roots and TMPDIR were under my own `/tmp` prefix; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `-B` and an empty PATH were used for checker execution. In-memory compile/3.9 AST checks avoid writing bytecode. Git commands were read-only, bounded `rev-parse`, `status`, `diff`, `ls-tree`, `show` and `cat-file` operations. Parser execution did not invoke Git.

These are distinct dispositions, not 292 asserted passing tests. Maximum observed process duration 0.8533 seconds, 15-second deadlines. |

Maximum 0.3094 seconds, 15-second deadlines. |

Their applicable dispositions remain as recorded in INITIAL; none is silently skipped or converted into a global resource/TOCTOU guarantee. No unchanged runtime/QEMU/Miri test is represented as necessary evidence for this host-only change.

## Final eight-axis checklist — every literal prompt individually updated

The standard uses unnumbered bullets. S01–S32 preserve its actual group/order and the review ordinals already declared above; M33 remains the distinct master-plan prompt. Literal kernel N/A does not waive the stated host analogue.

### 1. Capability correctness

**Axis outcome: OK for applicable host authority; literal capability transfer/revocation mechanisms unchanged.**

```text
[S01] Every privileged operation introduced by this change requires a capability.
```

**OK —** No new kernel privileged operation. Host reads remain limited to the selected public corpus; current audit executions observed no write, socket or process action.

```text
[S02] The capability required is the **narrowest** one that permits the operation (no privilege escalation by accepting a broad capability where a narrow one would do).
```

**OK —** Five root entry files plus docs/skills Markdown and the same three exact local-only exclusions remain the narrow host corpus; all three committed 387-path inventories match, with no archive exemptions.

```text
[S03] Capabilities are checked **before** observable side effects. A failed check does not leak information that the call was attempted.
```

**OK —** Source alias and target containment/type checks precede content opening; corrected-source unread-canary and special-file controls pass. Directory metadata may be inspected, and no race-free snapshot or universal metadata secrecy is promised.

```text
[S04] Capability transfer (if any) is move-only; no accidental cloning.
```

**N/A —** No capability or IPC transfer; copying Link/Finding metadata does not convey kernel authority or cause an external action.

```text
[S05] Capability duplication (if intended) itself requires an explicit `Duplicate`-style authority.
```

**N/A —** No capability duplication operation; repeated links repeat validation rather than mint authority.

```text
[S06] Capability revocation (if applicable) takes effect atomically; no partial state where the revocation is half-applied.
```

**N/A —** No revocation or concurrent kernel state changes. A sequential read-only host scan still has documented filesystem-race limitations.

### 2. Trust boundaries

**Axis outcome: OK after closure of all four host-integrity findings; negative controls verify target identity rather than a merely green corpus.**

```text
[S07] Every input crossing from userspace to the kernel is validated before use.
```

**OK —** Literal userspace/kernel validation is unchanged. Applicable document-to-result trust boundary defects SF01/SF02/C01/TABLE01 are corrected and independently closed with actual/decoy and full-diagnostic controls.

```text
[S08] Pointers from userspace are **never** dereferenced in kernel mode without going through a validated mapping.
```

**OK —** No kernel pointer. Corrected-source path/alias/private-prefix canaries verify the host validated-read analogue without reading private contents.

```text
[S09] Buffer lengths provided by userspace are range-checked against the actual mapped region.
```

**OK —** No mapped user buffer. Host read size is checked at 16 MiB+1 with a 16 MiB acceptance bound; nesting/label bounds and oversized controls still fail visibly. No total-memory theorem is claimed.

```text
[S10] Message contents from userspace are parsed into typed structures; no raw bytes are consumed in privileged code.
```

**OK —** Typed Link/Finding/TableCell/TableRow structures retain source context and raw offsets. Explicit URL cleanup, physical lines and cell isolation now preserve the intended target authority; malformed recognized forms retain diagnostics.

```text
[S11] Cross-task IPC does not grant the receiver any authority the sender did not have or did not explicitly transfer.
```

**N/A —** No IPC or task authority transfer. Host external URIs remain unfetched; documents cannot invoke subprocess/Git/settings operations in executed audit paths.

### 3. Memory safety

**Axis outcome: OK for the applicable host read/value boundaries; no Rust unsafe or native memory-safety proof is implied.**

```text
[S12] Any new `unsafe` block meets [unsafe-policy.md](unsafe-policy.md).
```

**N/A —** No new Rust unsafe block, runtime change or audit annotation in the 90-path delta. Host fixture success is not unsafe sign-off.

```text
[S13] Invariants stated in `# Safety` sections hold for every call site, not only "the usual" one.
```

**N/A —** No changed Rust # Safety contract. Host read preconditions and source-context invariants are checked by the actual containment, diagnostic and composition probes; all four host integrity findings are closed.

```text
[S14] No uninitialized memory is exposed. Where a buffer is declared but only partially filled, the unused portion is zeroed or the buffer is returned as `MaybeUninit`.
```

**OK —** No native uninitialized buffer. Length-bounded UTF-8 reads fail visibly on invalid input, physical offsets remain correct, and private/outside canaries do not enter output.

```text
[S15] No use-after-free. Lifetimes on raw pointers are reasoned about explicitly.
```

**N/A —** No raw pointer, FFI or runtime lifetime change. Python-managed values do not establish a proof of CPython internals.

```text
[S16] No aliasing violations. Mutable pointers to the same memory do not coexist.
```

**N/A —** No mutable raw-pointer alias. Filesystem aliases are separately exercised and rejected where required; they are not conflated with Rust memory aliasing.

### 4. Kernel-mode discipline

**Axis outcome: OK for bounded host execution tested; no kernel, scheduler or global exhaustion contract changes.**

```text
[S17] No allocation in interrupt service routines.
```

**N/A —** No ISR change. Applicable host allocation is addressed through bounded reads/depth and rerun large-input controls.

```text
[S18] No unbounded loops in kernel mode; every loop has a documented termination.
```

**OK —** No kernel loop. Host scanners advance offsets over bounded inputs; original large title/backtick/numeric cases and new 1000-row/2000-backtick compositions terminate under deadlines. No global linear-time/resource guarantee is asserted.

```text
[S19] Critical sections are minimized. Scheduler state is held for as short a time as possible.
```

**N/A —** No scheduler state or critical-section change; the delivered host scan is sequential.

```text
[S20] No new kernel panic introduced on a hot path.
```

**OK —** No kernel panic path. Executed malformed, UTF-8, absent-root, symlink-loop, size/depth and special-read cases yield typed findings/nonzero status without uncaught parser exceptions. Global MemoryError/OS exhaustion remains outside the promised contract.

```text
[M33] Every allocation path returns a typed error, not a panic, on exhaustion.
```

**N/A for kernel allocation; applicable host limits explicitly assessed —** No allocator/kernel path changed. Bounded reads/nesting and typed syntax/path/read findings were rerun; Python MemoryError or OS termination on global exhaustion remains possible and is not promised recoverable. No process-wide integer-limit setting changed. This is a declared host-contract limit, not a waived kernel obligation or a new current blocker.

### 5. Cryptography

**Axis outcome: N/A to delivered cryptography; standard evidence hashing is appropriate.**

```text
[S21] No roll-your-own primitives. If a primitive is introduced, it is a well-known algorithm from a reviewed crate (or, if truly novel, it comes with a separate ADR and a cited proof or analysis).
```

**N/A —** No crypto primitive/protocol delivered. Evidence SHA-256 uses standard hashlib rather than an invented algorithm.

```text
[S22] Keys are never logged, returned in error messages, or exposed via `Debug`.
```

**N/A —** No key input/object or crypto error channel. Applicable host diagnostics contain source locations/destinations; guarded private/outside contents remain unread.

```text
[S23] Constant-time comparisons are used where timing leaks matter.
```

**N/A —** No secret-dependent authentication/comparison; equality of public paths, anchors and evidence hashes does not require constant time.

```text
[S24] Randomness comes from an acceptable source — not `rand::thread_rng` with no seeding story.
```

**N/A —** The parser uses no randomness; harness temporary names are isolation devices, not cryptographic tokens.

```text
[S25] Nonces, IVs, and salts are handled correctly per the primitive's contract.
```

**N/A —** No nonce, IV, salt or replay-state contract changes.

### 6. Secrets and logging

**Axis outcome: OK for applicable host output/content-read behavior, subject to stated public-source and filesystem-race limits.**

```text
[S26] Secrets (keys, tokens, capability bits) are not included in logs, panic messages, debug output, or error types.
```

**OK —** No credential/token/environment-secret dump or private-content output channel. Current audit and rerun canary controls confirm no guarded read/write event. Secrets deliberately placed in public Markdown are not promised redaction.

```text
[S27] `Debug` impls on security-sensitive types redact sensitive fields (or are not implemented at all).
```

**N/A —** No Rust Debug implementation or secret-bearing type. JSON emits documented report metadata and findings, not internal process or secret state.

### 7. Dependencies

**Axis outcome: OK; stdlib source-context semantics now explicit and dual-interpreter controls pass.**

```text
[S28] Any new dependency has been evaluated under [infrastructure.md](infrastructure.md) dependency-addition rules.
```

**OK —** Only Python 3.9+ stdlib; no dependency/lockfile/install delta. Current executions succeed with empty PATH and forbidden subprocess/socket actions guarded.

```text
[S29] The dependency's trust model is understood. A build-time-only crate is very different from a kernel-linked crate.
```

**OK —** Host-only stdlib trust is explicit. SF01 no longer delegates source identity to version-sensitive urlsplit normalization; raw HTML/entity and physical-line rules are owned by the parser and tested on both actual interpreters.

```text
[S30] `cargo-vet` trust decisions are updated accordingly.
```

**N/A —** No Rust dependency or cargo-vet trust decision changes. Planned conditional cargo-vet/audit gates are not claimed as executed.

### 8. Threat-model impact

**Axis outcome: OK within the unchanged kernel model and declared host-gate integrity scope.**

```text
[S31] The change is reconciled with the documented threat model in [`docs/architecture/security-model.md`](../architecture/security-model.md).
```

**OK —** Unchanged pre-alpha capability threat model; the applicable added surface is host evidence integrity. All four demonstrated host-integrity issues are closed without implying a new kernel security certification.

```text
[S32] If the change reshapes the threat model, the PR includes the threat-model update or links to a follow-up PR scheduled imminently.
```

**OK —** No kernel threat-model reshaping. Bounded offline host behavior remains within Accepted ADR-0043; no architectural exception or model update is needed for these corrections.

## Final architectural principles P1–P12

| Principle | Current individual disposition |
|---|---|
| P1 — No ambient authority | OK: selected-root/public-prefix host reads, unread canaries and guarded offline/no-write execution; no kernel authority added. |
| P2 — Smallest defensible trusted computing base | OK: stdlib-only host parser remains outside kernel TCB; source-context normalization is explicit and verified. |
| P3 — Drivers, filesystems, and network stacks live in userspace | N/A: no runtime service placement changes; host filesystem access does not move a filesystem into the kernel. |
| P4 — Capability checks at every trust boundary | Kernel unchanged; host containment passes and the four document-to-result integrity defects are closed with full-diagnostic/actual-target evidence. |
| P5 — Rust-only for kernel code | OK: Python remains approved ADR-0043 host tooling, with no non-Rust kernel/HAL/service delta. |
| P6 — HAL separation | N/A: no CPU/register/board/HAL changes. |
| P7 — No proprietary binary blobs in the kernel | OK: no binary dependency/blob/build artifact introduced; evidence is text. |
| P8 — Decisions are recorded as ADRs | OK: Accepted ADR-0043 and the approved Step 11/12 plan govern this source; no architectural exception/status promotion. |
| P9 — English in the repository, Turkish in chat | OK: source, tests, account, task and review are English; historical quoted bytes remain preserved. |
| P10 — Mermaid for diagrams | N/A: no diagram added or binary-diagram substitution. |
| P11 — Reproducibility from the toolchain up | Kernel-build property unchanged; both actual compatible interpreters/cwds give identical current corpus output and expected target decisions. No hosted/binary reproducibility or cargo gate claim. |
| P12 — No half-finished subsystems on main | OK at development-only boundary: no merge/publication, T056 remains In Progress/all 16 unchecked, Step 12 and maintainer delivery gates stay pending. |

## Final T056 criterion traceability

Every full literal criterion is repeated below with an individual current disposition. No checkbox is completed by this security review; Step 11 support remains narrower than whole-task delivery.

```text
[AC1] Implement a Python 3.9+ standard-library command under tools/ with explicit UTF-8, script-relative default root, isolated fixture-root override, sorted deterministic file:line findings, zero only on success and nonzero for invalid input/inconsistency. No third-party dependency, network or repository-setting mutation. Document usage, exit codes, supported grammar/limits, interpreter requirement, adding checks and Required enforcement in docs/guides/ci.md and docs/standards/infrastructure.md. The delivered CI step is the selected ongoing index-consistency procedure; no new sync skill is needed.
```

**Step 11 supported: actual 3.9-compatible stdlib/UTF-8/script-relative root/fixture override/sorted findings/exits and guarded offline/no-write behavior pass; target/line blockers closed. Whole AC remains pending Step 12 infrastructure/Required procedure delivery.**

```text
[AC2] Local links: check docs/**/*.md, README.md, CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, SECURITY.md, CONTRIBUTING.md and .agents/skills/**/*.md; resolve files/directories and fragments. Document/test real inline/reference links and images, escaped/balanced/angle destinations, URL decoding, same-file/duplicate/Unicode headings, HTML anchors, source #L references and code-span/fence exclusions. Diagnose relevant unsupported forms rather than silently dropping them. External URIs are not fetched.
```

**Step 11 supported: exact public 387-file corpus, supported link/image/reference/entity/escape/URL/heading/HTML/source-line/code/table controls pass. All four demonstrated semantic blockers closed; relevant recognized unsupported forms remain diagnosed. No universal CommonMark/browser renderer is claimed; whole AC remains unchecked.**

```text
[AC3] Task indexes: exact T-*.md membership, duplicate detection, normalized lifecycle-status agreement; validate date_done when present and require it for newly completed tasks under the adopted convention without fabricating old dates.
```

**Step 12 pending: exact task membership/lifecycle/date_done enforcement is not delivered by this parser. Existing inventory evidence does not implement that family.**

```text
[AC4] Review/ADR indexes: exact canonical membership with duplicates/missing/extra findings; distinguish companions, nested tracks, master-review runs, README/master plans. Verify ADR status/date including Deferred, prose-extended dates and partial supersession; reserved 0034 requires no file.
```

**Step 12 pending: no canonical review/ADR membership checker. Reserved 0034 still requires no file, and full prior ordered ADR coverage remains valid.**



**Step 12 pending for the optional reliable live-state decision/implementation. Static commit-scope allowlisting remains explicitly skipped under ADR-0043, not silently accepted as enforced.**

```text
[AC9] Meaningful isolated positive/negative fixtures cover every implemented check, supported grammar and false-positive control, operating in temporary roots without scratch branches or committed regressions. The stabilized whole corpus passes before CI enablement; genuine baseline defects return to their named owner rather than being suppressed. ## R2-SF02 reopened — Medium — LF-only continuation and blank matchers
```

Frozen-eb5 locations are [BLANK_LINE/SPACES at 342–343](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L342), [close_label/spaces](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L388), [title_end](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L465) and the [same-offset CR normalization at 890](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L890). Lone CR is normalized, but CRLF retains its CR byte while the two regexes only recognize LF. This is incomplete propagation of the already selected physical-line model.

With a real `target.txt`, `[x](\r\n target.txt)\r\n` and `[x](target.txt\r\n "title")\r\n` wrongly emit line-1 malformed-link findings on both interpreters. The corresponding reference definition continuation emits undefined-reference line 1 and missing-destination line 3. Equivalent LF and CR controls pass. My additional image form `![x](\r\n target.txt)\r\n` has the same false positive.

More seriously, the exact source `[x](target.txt "first\r\n\r\n[real](missing.md)\r\nlast")\r\n` returns exit 0 with no findings on both interpreters. Equivalent LF/CR source emits the expected line-1 blank-separated-title diagnostic and the real `missing.md` path finding at line 3. A blank-separated CRLF label also fabricates a cross-paragraph link while LF/CR correctly leave prose. These are supported-context integrity faults, not a request to implement a whole renderer. The actual read [CommonMark link grammar](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#links) permits a line ending between link components and disallows blank lines in titles; its [line model](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#characters-and-lines) includes CRLF. This fixture has no interrupting heading/fence, so the earlier withdrawn title expectation does not dispose of it.

**Required correction:** make continuation and blank-boundary consumers use the intended LF/CR/CRLF semantics while preserving original offsets. Add exact positive inline/image/reference continuation cases and negative blank-title/label cases for all three endings, with full physical diagnostics and the real downstream missing target. Retain original Unicode-scalar, source-line, HTML/error-offset and actual-target regressions. SF02 remains open until the committed fix is independently verified.

## ROOT-R2-TABLE01 reopened — Medium — parent comment masking crosses a table boundary

Frozen-eb5 [table erasure in blocks](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L275) removes rows from the parent's parsing view; [comments](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L547), invoked at [899](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L899), then extends an incomplete inline opener to the end when the erased table contained its apparent closer. Child cell isolation alone does not protect later parent prose.

Minimal README, with `missing.md` absent:

```markdown
prose <!--
| A | B |
| --- | --- |
| text | text |
-->

[real](missing.md)
```

Both exact 6f9 runs report `README.md:7`, code `path`, message `Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md`, exit 1. Both eb5 runs return zero targets, no findings and exit 0. When a second broken link is placed in row 4, 6f9 misses that table link but reports line 7; eb5 reports line 4 but misses line 7. Neither version satisfies the two-link oracle, and a nonzero exit would conceal the changed omission.

I checked the actual primary rules before accepting this semantic interpretation. [GFM tables](https://github.github.com/gfm/#tables-extension-) parse cells as inlines and separate the table from following blocks. The official [cmark-gfm table implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/github/cmark-gfm/master/extensions/table.c), actually inspected at lines 225–370, tracks a preceding paragraph offset and inserts that paragraph separately before making the table. This supports the code review's paragraph-split interpretation; I did not install or execute a renderer or audit that entire library. The [CommonMark raw-comment grammar](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#raw-html), actually read at lines 8114–8143, also distinguishes a complete inline comment from an incomplete opener and recognizes the short comment forms.

Coordinator supplemental cases expose the related existing masking defect: a cell containing `text <!-- [real](missing.md)` hides a real line-3 link on both versions; `<!-->` and `<!--->` also consume following links instead of ending. An incomplete prose opener before a blank line hides a real line-3 HTML href on both versions. My additional `prose <!-- [real](missing.md)` and `prose <!--> <a href="missing.md">real</a>` both silently pass at eb5 rather than reporting line 1. These are host result-integrity failures in the same comment/context family, not new kernel findings or all new table regressions.

Controls are material: escaped parent opener, actual block-start comment, ordinary complete inline comment and complete same-cell comment preserve expected behavior in both revisions; my unclosed block-start control stays literal. A repair that indiscriminately exposes real block-comment contents would regress that boundary.

**Required correction:** coordinate parent block boundaries with inline/comment/HTML masking, and distinguish complete inline comments (including short forms) from incomplete literal openers and actual block-start comments. Preserve real subsequent prose/HTML targets, child cell isolation, physical locations and original source ranges. Add the exact old-pass/new-fail case plus full two-target, escaped/block/non-table/incomplete/short-form controls before changing source. No table/archive exclusion or broad syntax suppression is acceptable. TABLE01 remains open.

## Findings and old-finding disposition

**R2-SF03 — Medium — Closing-line real HTML navigation is hidden by synthetic code indentation.** At [tools/docs_consistency.py:329](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L329), the block-comment bytes become spaces, then the resulting line is classified again for indentation; [line 339](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L339) drops the entire line as indented code. The spaces came from the erased comment, not source code indentation. Minimal public README:

```html
<!-- c --> <a href="missing.md">real</a>
```

With no `missing.md`, f811 returns `ok: true`, zero targets/findings, exit 0. Expected is exactly `README.md:1`, code `path`, message `Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md`, exit 1. A two-line `<!--` then `--> <img src="missing.md">` similarly drops the real line-2 source. Both forms reproduce on LF, CR and CRLF, both interpreters; both already fail at eb5. A separate following line containing the same HTML link is diagnosed correctly. Ordinary Markdown text after a block comment's closer is raw HTML text and is correctly not treated as Markdown navigation in the tested control; the remedy must not convert it into Markdown links.

The guide explicitly promises actual HTML href/src checking and comment exclusion without source-line drift. The [CommonMark HTML-block rule](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#html-blocks) keeps the closing physical line in raw HTML; an HTML tag after the comment closer remains outside that comment. Action: retain closing-line raw HTML metadata/navigation while preventing synthetic spaces from creating code classification; add exact-path/line positive and negative controls for href/src and anchors, same-line versus next-line, and all physical endings. This is a checker-integrity defect, not a request for a complete renderer or a kernel exploit claim.

**R2-SF04 — Medium — A noninterrupting ordered marker breaks a complete inline comment and fabricates anchors.** [tools/docs_consistency.py:378](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L378) treats every list marker as a new block; the new [CommentSpans end check at line 417](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L417) inherits that boundary. Minimal README:

```markdown
prose <!--
2. <span id="ghost"></span> -->

[x](#ghost)
```

Here `2.` continues the existing paragraph, so the span is inside a complete inline comment and does not define `ghost`. Expected is exactly `README.md:4`, code `fragment`, message `Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: ghost`, exit 1. At eb5 this is diagnosed; f811 returns no findings/exit 0 by admitting the commented-out anchor. Replacing the span with `[literal](missing.md)` gives the complementary new false positive at line 2. Both effects reproduce on LF/CR/CRLF and both interpreters. The `1.` interruption control is now correctly checked by f811; the ordinary soft-text comment control remains hidden. The [CommonMark list-item rule](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#list-items) permits only ordered start 1 to interrupt a paragraph. Action: derive comment boundaries from actual container/paragraph transitions instead of the presence of any syntactic list marker; preserve valid complete comments across soft continuation while retaining the real `1.`/heading/table/container interruption protections. Add both the fragment false-green and literal-link false-positive regressions, with exact diagnostics and line endings.

The primary rules cited above were actually opened and checked during this verification. I did not install or execute an external CommonMark renderer. A separate spaced thematic-break composition was validated against the primary thematic-break rule and passed at f811. The speculative closing-line-fence cases all preserved the following real link in actual execution and are explicitly not findings.

| R2-SF02 physical lines, including reopened CRLF continuation/blank-line forms | Targeted closure verified: all disclosed and supplemental CRLF oracles pass current source; no CRLF waiver. |
| R2-C01 multiline HTML metadata | Targeted closure retained through earlier exact matrix and current original/composition controls. SF03 concerns closing-line block-comment masking, separately reported. |
| ROOT-R2-TABLE01 cells and reopened parent-comment crossing | Disclosed cases close under exact current controls, including full finding lists; SF04 exposes a further non-table paragraph-boundary defect and keeps overall comment integrity on HOLD. |
| R2-SF03 closing-line HTML | Open Medium, baseline and current false green. |
| R2-SF04 ordered soft continuation | Open Medium, new f811 false green and false positive. |

## Confirmed disposition of the newly disclosed families

**SF02 reopened, Medium — physical boundaries can still be skipped through a backslash.** The earlier exact CRLF-whitespace repairs remain actual passes, but [close_label at f811 line 432](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L432) and [title_end at line 507](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L507) advance over any following character before observing blank separation. A backslash immediately before LF or normalized lone CR hides the blank-title diagnostic and the real missing link on physical line 3; the matching label form fabricates cross-paragraph navigation. The peer's no-backslash and escaped-punctuation controls pass, as do the CRLF forms in this specific matrix. The prior narrower SF02 closure must not be read as closure of this newly supplied composition.

The coordinator independently extended this to [destination at f811 line 471](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L471): backslash-newline and backslash-space can bypass destination syntax restrictions and select an existing deliberately malformed destination decoy. Lone-CR normalization can instead produce an incorrect path finding, which still fails the required syntax oracle; nonzero exit alone does not validate that case. The source's broad `isspace()` also rejects valid non-ASCII filename content while admitting some forbidden raw ASCII controls. These attributed coordinator cases substantiate the same source-grammar validation barrier and require shared-consumer correction, not an isolated title regex patch.

**SF04 shared-boundary extension, Medium — nonblank Unicode/control lines become blank boundaries.** The coordinator's nine scalar forms are content under the stated physical-line/blank-line grammar, but [CodeSpans at f811 line 378](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L378) uses broad `strip()` and broad whitespace marker tests. CommentSpans inherits the resulting fake boundary and exposes comment contents. This is distinct from the ordered `2.` trigger I originally found, but shares its source-to-boundary failure. The supplied 54 current scalar-comment cases all fail their correct empty-findings oracle.

My additional independent controls replace the hidden link with `<span id="ghost"></span>` and later `[x](#ghost)`. With either VT or NBSP as the intervening content line, f811 falsely accepts the line-6 fragment on both interpreters; eb5 correctly reports `Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: ghost`. A true ASCII space/tab blank-line control correctly exposes the anchor at f811, while eb5 incorrectly hides it. The fix therefore must distinguish actual blank syntax from ordinary Unicode content rather than simply keeping every comment open. These twelve current/historical observations are in `additional-controls.json` and do not turn the entire coordinator expansion into a reviewer-original discovery.

The actual primary [backslash rules](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#backslash-escapes), [destination/title rules](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#links), and previously checked [physical/blank-line definitions](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#characters-and-lines) support these oracles: punctuation escaping does not erase physical boundaries; raw destination syntax and decoded entity content have distinct constraints; a non-ASCII whitespace scalar is not an ASCII blank line. The checker contract diagnoses recognized malformed syntax even where a renderer would leave literal text. No installed renderer or full-rendering claim is involved.

## Read-only repair advice for SF03 and current review effect

For SF03, retain the **closing physical line as raw HTML context**. The literal Markdown labels/backticks/fence text on that line must not be parsed as Markdown, while actual id/name/href/src after the comment closer must remain visible to HTML processing. Do not reinterpret spaces manufactured by comment masking as real indentation, and do not send the remainder unqualified through the normal Markdown inline pass.

Two mixed controls were independently executed on both interpreters: `<!-- c --> [literal](missing.md) <a href="real-missing.md">real</a>` must report only the actual HTML target, and raw closing-line backticks around that HTML tag do not create a Markdown code span. All four current executions omit the real target, confirming SF03. The closing-line ` ``` ` plus a following real Markdown link control passes both current executions: the text on the raw line must not open a fence that swallows the following link. These six observations, together with the original same/next-line and quote/list tests, make the requested correction concrete without prescribing a full renderer.

## Confirmed current findings and repair advice

**R2-C02 — Medium, attributed to the CODE reviewer: incorrect setext paragraph boundaries fabricate anchors and reject real anchors.** In [the heading scan](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1243), especially the backward accumulation at lines 1255–1258, nonblank preceding bodies are joined without respecting the actual paragraph/container boundary, a completed heading extent, or an intervening thematic break. For `1. First\n2. Second\n   ---\n\n[x](#first-second)\n`, the current CLI returns success with no findings; the actual heading is `Second`, so the expected finding is `README.md:5 fragment: Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: first-second`. Changing the link to `#second` wrongly rejects that real anchor. `First\n---\nSecond\n---\n\n[x](#first-----second)\n` similarly accepts a fabricated anchor; the real second heading must stand independently. These failures occur in retained f811 and current 457 source, rather than constituting a newly introduced 457 regression. Coordinator extensions independently confirm outdented/lazy underline and thematic-break boundaries. Real multiline headings, an actual blank, and an ATX boundary remain passing controls.

**SF04 — Medium, reopened for retained nested lazy quote/list composition, attributed current counterexamples to the CODE reviewer.** In [the partially matched container branch](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L157), especially lines 168–172, an omitted inner quote is lost when the outer list still matches; the fallback preserves the full active chain only for the narrower outdented-list condition. For `- > prose <!--\n  2. <span id="ghost"></span> -->\n\n[x](#ghost)\n`, current output is success with no findings. The span is literal within the continuing comment, so `README.md:4 fragment: Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: ghost` is required. The corresponding missing-path and cross-line-code cases wrongly expose literal targets. These nested gaps exist in f811 as well. The original top-level lazy-quote repair remains effective: the current top-level control passes, while the old version fails it. That limited repair cannot justify closing the nested composition gap. Explicit quote continuation, ordered-1 interruption and genuine blank controls retain their distinct correct outcomes.

**Coordinator extension to C02/SF04: ineligible lazy `===` falsely ends inline state.** For `> prose <!--\n===\n[literal](missing.md) -->\n`, no target should be emitted, but both source versions report `README.md:3 path: Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md`. The same outcome is confirmed for list/nested comments and a quote code span. Explicit `> ===` and a real ASCII blank correctly expose the later real target. This is an attributed coordinator case, not a new independent reviewer discovery or a newly introduced regression. The shared boundary decision must distinguish an eligible underline from lazy paragraph content before clearing comment/code state.

Repair these related boundary decisions using a bounded shared representation of the actual open paragraph, its container identity and its consumed heading extent. Once an eligible underline closes that paragraph, retain its own source span and consume it once for slugging; do not rescan all previous nonblank bodies. Preserve legitimate soft multiline headings and duplicate suffixes. Preserve omitted inner quote markers only while a real paragraph continues inside the matching outer container; actual blank lines, new sibling items and interrupting blocks must still end it. Keep original offsets and physical-line diagnostics, and recheck raw HTML, tables, fences, references and inline masks against that same decision. This advice asks for the supported bounded helper/event solution, not a complete Markdown renderer or a new ADR.

There is also a confirmed bounded resource consequence of the same unbounded-prefix accumulation. My 200/400-heading runs measured approximately 0.138/0.542 seconds on Python 3.9 and 0.093/0.365 seconds on Python 3.13; all returned the requested count of anchors but lacked the correct last anchor. The retained 5,000-heading selected test reaches its own five-second child deadline on both interpreters, producing `TimeoutExpired` unittest errors. These are measured observations and a repair-motivating scan problem, not a new universal performance policy, assertion failure, or proof of global denial-of-service limits.

## Confirmed Medium finding: C02 tab/container boundary regression

**Medium — independent SECURITY extension of R2-C02; current supported-heading correctness blocks scoped acceptance.** For this exact source, where `\t` denotes an actual tab:

```text
-\tAlpha
    ---

[x](#alpha)
```

The expected findings are empty with exit 0: `Alpha` is the list item's setext heading. Retained 457 produces that result. Current e018285 returns exit 1 with exactly `README.md:4 fragment: Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: alpha`. Both Python versions reproduce it with LF, CR and CRLF. This is a newly introduced regression, distinct from retained tab-related gaps described below. It is a false rejection of valid supported navigation; these probes do not establish secret access, native memory corruption or an external filesystem escape.

The [CommonMark 0.31.2 tab rules and examples](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#tabs) were actually checked. Tabs affect block structure at four-column stops; applying those rules with the already checked list/setext rules makes the tab after the marker equivalent to the appropriate structural padding. This semantic oracle is an inference from the primary rules and examples, not output from an installed renderer. Literal inline tab data must remain source data.

The defect is the mismatch between raw-character widths and visual container identity. [The prefix parser](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L130) records raw match lengths; [the block walker](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L452) obtains event parts from raw text while obtaining active parts from expanded visual text. [ParagraphState.record](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L231) then clears an otherwise continuing paragraph when these differently measured parts compare unequal. In the witness, the raw first-line list width is two characters while its visual width and the following four-space continuation are four columns. The underline loses its paragraph and the real anchor disappears. Continuation tabs expose adjacent existing raw/visual inconsistencies.

My six-family confirmation matrix retains every source, exact full diagnostic, exit, stderr, version, interpreter and ending: 72 executions, 36 current and 36 old. The marker-tab/four-space witness passes all six old combinations and fails all six current combinations. A tab in both marker padding and continuation, and a space-padded marker followed by a tab continuation, fail all six combinations in both versions: these are retained gaps, not newly introduced regressions. Four-space marker/continuation, ordinary two-space list continuation and quote-tab controls pass in both versions. Thus current has 18 mismatches / 18 passing controls; old has 12 mismatches / 24 passing controls. The earlier independent 144-run adjacent matrix has six failures for the both-tab family and 138 passing observations; it is not reported as 144 passes.

Repair the shared container representation so structural columns and source offsets are separate and consistently related. Preserve original source offsets for diagnostics and literal inline/URL/HTML data; do not globally expand or rewrite the source. Use the same visual container identity for preliminary/final paragraph decisions, explicit and lazy continuation, and heading eligibility. Add exact positive/negative marker-tab and continuation-tab tests, retaining ordinary spaces, nested lazy quote/list comments/code, real blanks and interrupting boundaries as controls. This is bounded repair advice for the declared grammar, not a request for complete renderer equivalence. Root has acknowledged the independently reported witness; its later corroboration counts are not claimed as my executions, and no repair or peer agreement is inferred.

## Confirmed findings and bounded repair advice

**SF04 — Medium, CODE-attributed retained outer-list sibling omission.** In frozen e018285 [block_body](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L168), the accumulated quote/list outdent flags feed the final decision at line 178. A quote that ended inside an outer list can wrongly keep a real sibling inside the previous paragraph's comment/code state. For `1. > prose <!--\n2. [real](missing.md) -->\n`, the expected finding is `README.md:2 path: Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md`, exit 1. Both 457 and e018285 instead emit a success report with zero targets and no findings. Cross-line code, `)` markers and two nested quotes reproduce the omission; the actual `<span id="real">` sibling is instead hidden, so a later real `#real` is rejected. This is retained behavior, not a newly introduced e018285 regression.

The primary [CommonMark list-item rules](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#list-items) and [cmark block implementation](https://github.com/commonmark/cmark/blob/master/src/blocks.c) were actually inspected. A failed item continuation can leave its enclosing list matched; a new sibling is opened before the fallback lazy-paragraph decision. Applying that structure to these sources is my stated inference, not execution of an installed renderer. Preserve which container level remains matched and whether its existing parent list accepts a sibling. Do not make every non-1 marker interrupt a paragraph: the three-space-indented inner-lazy control now passes e018285, and explicit quote soft-marker, plain-list sibling and blank-separated sibling controls distinguish the required cases.

**R2-C02 — Medium, CODE-attributed heading-text mask defect, including one new phantom-anchor regression.** The exact heading `# Alpha<!--x-->Beta` must yield `alphabeta`. Both revisions reject that real anchor and accept the nonexistent `alpha--------beta`. Tags similarly make `# Alpha<span>Beta</span>` accept `alpha------beta`; setext forms also fail. At frozen module [line 777](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L777), comment bytes become offset-preserving spaces, which reach heading input at line 1096; HTML heading masks at line 1260 add placeholders, and trimming/joining at lines 1318/1324 can turn removed multiline content into a separator. Diagnostic masks are being mistaken for visible heading text.

For `Alpha<!--\nx\n-->Beta\n---\n\n[x](#alpha-beta)\n`, expected `README.md:6 fragment: Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: alpha-beta` is correctly reported by 457 but newly omitted by e018285. Both still reject the actual `alphabeta`. Real spaces surrounding a multiline comment must remain real spaces: the `alpha--beta` and duplicate suffix controls establish why globally collapsing whitespace would be incorrect. The primary [GitHub section-link rules](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/writing-on-github/getting-started-with-writing-and-formatting-on-github/basic-writing-and-formatting-syntax#section-links) and [CommonMark raw-HTML grammar](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#raw-html) were actually read: markup and comment tokens do not manufacture visible text or separators. The exact slug oracles are an application of those rules, not a claim of complete renderer equivalence.

Keep diagnostic/source offsets while building heading content from source spans whose provenance distinguishes removed markup, literal protected code and genuine spaces/soft breaks. Removed comment/tag spans contribute no characters; genuine source spaces, duplicates and code contents retain their meanings. Pair every real-anchor assertion with its phantom-anchor counterpart, including multiline comments, physical endings, escapes and inline code. This correction can remain bounded to the documented grammar and shared event/span representation.

**R2-C02/SF04 tab extension — Medium, original tab witness independently SECURITY-attributed; new compositions coordinator-attributed.** My original marker-tab/four-space heading regression and retained continuation-tab gaps remain confirmed. The coordinator's additional source `-\tprose <!--\n\t<span id="ghost"></span> -->\n\n[x](#ghost)\n` also exposes an actual new false green: 457 correctly rejects `#ghost` at line 4, but e018285 accepts the literal comment's fabricated anchor. Analogous tabbed comment and code continuations wrongly expose a literal missing link on line 2 only in e018285. The two-space outdented underline remains an important opposite control: e018285 correctly rejects its nonexistent heading, improving on 457. These newly supplied compositions extend the earlier independently observed tab failure; they are not retroactively claimed as my pre-disclosure discoveries.

The tab repair still requires one consistent structural-column/container identity with an original-source offset mapping. It must preserve both matched-parent sibling decisions and literal inline state. Global tab expansion, unconditional non-1 interruption, wholesale whitespace collapse and broad corpus exclusions would each discard a passing control. The earlier bounded consecutive-heading scan remains repaired in its measured cases; no new unbounded renderer audit or performance policy is requested.

### Medium C02 extension — heading projection changes inline semantics

The current projection removes markup and trims physical lines before `slug()` reparses inline text. It preserves visible characters but not all source context needed to decide their meaning. Relevant source: [heading projection](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1356), particularly physical trimming at lines 1,371–1,374 and reparsing at line 1,400; zero-width comment/tag spans are projected before that reparse. This affects the explicitly supported heading/code/emphasis contract in the [current guide](../../../guides/ci.md#documentation-consistency), not an unstated demand for a complete renderer.

**Retained code-whitespace defect:** the following literal source has two spaces after `a` inside the code span (the escaped representation makes them explicit):

```text
"Alpha `a  \nb`\n---\n\n[x](#alpha-a---b)\n"
```

The code span contains `a`, two literal spaces, a physical break, and `b`; normalizing the break adds one space. The true heading anchor is `alpha-a---b`. Both e018 and 8ed wrongly return `README.md:5: fragment: Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: alpha-a---b`. Replacing only the destination with `#alpha-a-b` wrongly returns exit0, one checked local target/fragment and no findings on both revisions. An equivalent one-line code span with three internal spaces and a multiline code span with no extra spaces are passing controls. This is not newly attributed to 8ed.

**New phantom emphasis anchors:** for `# Alpha<!--x-->_Beta_` and `# Alpha<span></span>_Beta_`, the actual source punctuation before `_Beta_` permits emphasis; visible heading text is `AlphaBeta`. Current 8ed wrongly rejects `#alphabeta` at `README.md:3`, and newly accepts `#alpha_beta_` with exit0/no findings. e018 already rejected the real anchor but correctly rejected this particular phantom. Literal intraword `# Alpha_Beta_` correctly retains its underscores, and `# Alpha _Beta_` correctly produces `alpha-beta`; these controls prohibit fixing the issue by globally stripping underscores or collapsing whitespace.

The semantic oracles are inferences from primary rules actually read: [CommonMark code spans](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#code-spans) preserve internal spaces while normalizing physical breaks; [emphasis flanking](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#emphasis-and-strong-emphasis) depends on adjacent source punctuation; [GitHub section anchors](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/writing-on-github/getting-started-with-writing-and-formatting-on-github/basic-writing-and-formatting-syntax#section-links) remove formatting and replace spaces with hyphens. No external renderer was installed or executed. The delimiter repair was separately checked against [CommonMark lists, Example 302](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#lists).

`heading-confirmation.json` retains **120 actual CLI executions**, ten positive/negative source families × three physical endings × two revisions × both supported interpreters. Current8ed has 36 semantic mismatches and 24 passing controls; olde018 has 24 mismatches and 36 controls. Every record includes literal source, full expected findings, actual complete report, exit, stderr and exact command. These counts overlap in defect family and are not unique vulnerabilities. Six preceding direct `Markdown` exploratory samples were unscored reconnaissance; the finding rests on the retained CLI matrix and checked semantics.

Impact is Medium host-gate integrity and availability: an invalid local fragment can be accepted while its real target is rejected. No arbitrary code execution, filesystem escape or kernel-memory exploit is claimed. Root was notified with the exact artifact before completion and independently owns repair. Keep this C02 extension open. A bounded repair should determine code contents and emphasis from source context before final visible heading projection; carry protected whitespace and delimiter context through the existing paragraph extents, then apply slug rules to the resulting text. Retain zero-width HTML/comment behavior, original offsets, literal intraword/escaped underscores, code literals, actual spacing/soft breaks, duplicates and source-based ATX closers. Add positive and phantom-negative regressions first; do not replace this with global whitespace collapse or underscore deletion.

I actually checked [CommonMark paragraphs, Example222](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#paragraphs), and [the reference implementation's paragraph/lazy-line input handling](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/commonmark/cmark/master/src/blocks.c) at lines1,123–1,126 and1,174–1,188: leading indentation is removed before inline parsing. The [code-span normalization rule](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#code-spans) then preserves the remaining internal/trailing spaces. This distinction does not withdraw my original trailing-only120-case matrix.

### Confirmed finding R2-RESUME-SEC01 — heading projection merges distinct code-span delimiters

**Medium; host-gate integrity and availability. Retained from 8ed03cf; not newly introduced by 7b44ce9.** A document author can create a heading with two separate inline code spans divided by zero-width HTML or a comment. Projection removes the separator before code spans are parsed for the slug. Distinct source delimiter runs become one run, changing which spaces are normalized. The checker rejects a real section link and accepts a nonexistent section link. This extends the retained C02/heading projection family; it is not an arbitrary code execution, native memory corruption, privilege escalation or kernel finding.

Minimal exact UTF-8/LF source for the real anchor:

```markdown
# ` a `<!--x-->` b `

[x](#ab)
```

Expected complete report is `{"checks":["links"],"ok":true,"files":1,"local_targets":1,"fragments":1,"findings":[]}`, exit 0, empty stderr. Both exact revisions return exit 1, empty stderr and the same counters but `ok:false` with `{"path":"README.md","line":3,"code":"fragment","message":"Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: ab"}`. Replacing only the link with `[x](#a--b)` should produce the analogous missing-anchor finding for `a--b`, exit 1; both revisions instead return the empty-findings success report and exit 0. The phantom is therefore an observed false green, not merely a suspected display difference.

Source explanation: [inline_text](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py) at lines 956–990 accepts original offsets but constructs `CodeSpans(text)` at line 964 from the projected string. `_inline` records original code spans at line 1309; `_headings` lines 1380–1438 uses them to preserve trailing whitespace, while `project` removes `None` HTML/comment positions and passes the newly adjacent text through `slug`/`inline_text`. The new emphasis logic consults source offsets, but code-run identity still comes from projected adjacency. The two original code spans each normalize ` a ` or ` b ` to `a` or `b`; the comment contributes no visible separator. The correct visible text is `ab`.

The code-span parsing/edge-space normalization premise was checked against the primary [CommonMark 0.31.2 specification](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/). The anchor premise was checked against GitHub's documented [section links](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/writing-on-github/getting-started-with-writing-and-formatting-on-github/basic-writing-and-formatting-syntax#section-links). These are source-based semantic inferences combined with actual CLI observations; no GitHub renderer was installed or executed, and no full CommonMark equivalence is claimed.

Independent `adjacent-code-confirmation.json` records **108 exact CLI commands**: 2 revisions × 2 actual interpreters × LF/CR/CRLF × 9 cases. Each revision has 42 full-object mismatches and 12 positive controls. Families are comment-real/comment-phantom; equivalent `<span></span>` real/phantom; asymmetric `` `a`<!--x-->` b ` `` real `ab`/phantom `a-b-`; different code-run lengths with real `ab`; a separating-space control producing `a-b`; and a single literal code span producing `a--b`. All expected/actual dictionaries, counters, lines, exit statuses, stderr, exact source bytes/SHA and argv are retained. The misleading helper case name `different-run-control` is one of the failing real-anchor cases, not a passing positive control.

**Required repair:** preserve original code-span token boundaries and their normalization through heading projection, or render the source-parsed inline tokens before removing zero-width markup. Add tests first for real and phantom anchors across comment/tag boundaries, asymmetric edge spaces, distinct backtick run lengths and positive spacing/literal controls. Preserve the current source-aware emphasis, Markdown whitespace classes, paragraph-leading indentation removal, trailing-code whitespace, containers, tables and decoding behavior. Re-run the retained matrices and independent adjacent cases on an immutable new revision. Global whitespace collapse, widening exemptions or accepting both real and phantom slugs would not repair this finding.

### 1. Capability correctness

**OK for the host authority analogue; literal capability operations N/A as individually recorded below.** A malicious document has no product command-execution or network authority. File resolution is constrained to the supplied root, with excluded private prefixes, symlink/path/case checks and nonregular-file rejection before content use. Explicit `--root` grants the invoker a fixture scope; it is not a sandbox against the invoker. Audit canaries and source inspection verify the supported static filesystem model. No TOCTOU-resistant filesystem sandbox or revocation protocol is claimed.

### 2. Trust boundaries

**Flagged — R2-RESUME-SEC01.** Source Markdown/HTML is untrusted relative to the gate result; original delimiter boundaries must survive zero-width projection. A caller can currently obtain a false green for a phantom anchor and a false red for a real anchor. The existing URL decoding, entity/source identity, physical-line, table/container and raw-tail controls match their retained full oracles and own adjacent controls. Path/read boundaries pass the own audit cases. No userspace/kernel or IPC crossing is changed.

### 3. Memory safety

**N/A for native/kernel memory mechanics; OK for inspected host use within stated limits.** Python stdlib strings, indexes and typed records introduce no Rust unsafe/raw-pointer ownership or FFI changes. Range/nesting/source-index guards, size cap and malformed-input controls were read and exercised. Code-span projection loses semantic provenance, not native memory safety. No CPython or OS memory-safety audit is implied.

### 4. Kernel-mode discipline

**N/A for ISR/scheduler/kernel execution; flagged for host availability due to SEC01 false-red gate behavior.** Own bounded resource families complete under their deadlines; tests exercise explicit nesting/size/nonregular-file rejection. Deterministic errors prevent malformed public input from silently passing, subject to the confirmed heading defect. Existing operational read errors return findings. No universal CPU/memory/TOCTOU guarantee, controlled out-of-memory fault injection or typed Python allocation-error guarantee is claimed. The master-only allocation prompt M33 remains separately recorded below.

### 5. Cryptography

**N/A.** No cryptographic primitive, protocol, secret key, randomness contract or timing-sensitive comparison is introduced. SHA-256 hashes here bind review evidence bytes; they are not an application cryptosystem or authenticity/signature guarantee.

### 6. Secrets and logging

**OK for the host boundary; kernel-secret types N/A.** Diagnostics identify public source/targets and errors; no credential lookup, environment dump or private content opening occurs in the product controls. Excluded/private and outside-root aliases are rejected before their contents are read. Caller-authored public strings can appear in diagnostics as designed; the checker is not a redaction service for secrets a caller places in public documents. Own command evidence does not print secret environment values.

### 7. Dependencies

**OK.** The delta uses Python3.9+ standard library only. No package install, network dependency, native code addition, Cargo.lock change, cargo-vet policy change or CI/protection edit occurs. Actual dual-interpreter runs complement source/syntax review. `cargo-audit`/`cargo-vet` are not falsely described as executed or currently enforced by this task.

### 8. Threat-model impact

**Flagged for the host-gate integrity issue; no kernel threat-model expansion.** The [security model](../../../architecture/security-model.md) and architectural principles were read. Treating repository-controlled documents as input to a future required validation gate makes false acceptance and false rejection relevant host integrity/availability failures. SEC01 is bounded to that claim. This work does not repair T-038 HIGH findings, grant Phase C–D clearance, promote a task, or prove kernel isolation. No unsupported broader redesign/escalation is needed for the concrete parser correction.

### All 32 literal security-standard prompts, with individual disposition

The prompt text below is the security standard's checklist verbatim; relative link destinations are adjusted for this report directory. `N/A` always identifies the absent literal mechanism and its host analogue. These are SECURITY outcomes, not the five CODE roles.

**S01. Every privileged operation introduced by this change requires a capability.**

N/A — no kernel privileged operation is introduced; host authority is root-constrained read access, verified by source and the own audit harness.

**S02. The capability required is the **narrowest** one that permits the operation (no privilege escalation by accepting a broad capability where a narrow one would do).**

N/A — no capability type or acceptance rule changes; host reads use only explicit/default root and fail for private/outside targets.

**S03. Capabilities are checked **before** observable side effects. A failed check does not leak information that the call was attempted.**

N/A — no capability check changes; host path, private and nonregular-file checks precede content reads, as canary audits confirm.

**S04. Capability transfer (if any) is move-only; no accidental cloning.**

N/A — there is no capability transfer or cloning; product reads do not transfer OS authority.

**S05. Capability duplication (if intended) itself requires an explicit `Duplicate`-style authority.**

N/A — no capability duplication operation exists in these scripts; no new process or network authority is obtained.

**S06. Capability revocation (if applicable) takes effect atomically; no partial state where the revocation is half-applied.**

N/A — no revocation operation or concurrent authority state exists; static host path validation is not claimed as atomic TOCTOU defense.

**S07. Every input crossing from userspace to the kernel is validated before use.**

N/A literally; flagged host analogue — no userspace/kernel input path changes, but untrusted heading projection violates gate validation (SEC01).

**S08. Pointers from userspace are **never** dereferenced in kernel mode without going through a validated mapping.**

N/A — no userspace pointer is dereferenced; host source offsets use Python strings and bounded/indexed parsing.

**S09. Buffer lengths provided by userspace are range-checked against the actual mapped region.**

N/A — no mapped userspace buffer exists; host byte size and nesting limits are explicit and exercised.

**S10. Message contents from userspace are parsed into typed structures; no raw bytes are consumed in privileged code.**

N/A literally; flagged host analogue — no privileged IPC bytes exist, but structured host parse/report output misrepresents adjacent code spans (SEC01).

**S11. Cross-task IPC does not grant the receiver any authority the sender did not have or did not explicitly transfer.**

N/A — no cross-task IPC or authority transfer occurs; audit checks observe no external process/network side effects.

**S12. Any new `unsafe` block meets [unsafe-policy.md](../../../standards/unsafe-policy.md).**

N/A — no Rust unsafe block or native extension is added; the unsafe audit-log requirement is not triggered.

**S13. Invariants stated in `# Safety` sections hold for every call site, not only "the usual" one.**

N/A — no unsafe # Safety API/call site changes; host semantic source-context invariant is independently flagged as SEC01.

**S14. No uninitialized memory is exposed. Where a buffer is declared but only partially filled, the unused portion is zeroed or the buffer is returned as `MaybeUninit`.**

N/A — no uninitialized native buffer is managed; host bytes are read/decode-validated and rejected on invalid UTF-8.

**S15. No use-after-free. Lifetimes on raw pointers are reasoned about explicitly.**

N/A — no raw pointer lifetimes or FFI ownership appear; Python object lifetime is delegated to the existing interpreter.

**S16. No aliasing violations. Mutable pointers to the same memory do not coexist.**

N/A — no mutable raw-pointer aliasing mechanism appears; ordinary host list/string mutation does not claim a native proof.

**S17. No allocation in interrupt service routines.**

N/A — no ISR executes this code; host allocation occurs in a normal CLI process.

**S18. No unbounded loops in kernel mode; every loop has a documented termination.**

N/A literally — no kernel loop changes; host loops/indexes and bounded-resource tests were inspected, with no universal resource proof claimed.

**S19. Critical sections are minimized. Scheduler state is held for as short a time as possible.**

N/A — no scheduler state or critical section changes; the CLI has no added locking/concurrent shared kernel state.

**S20. No new kernel panic introduced on a hot path.**

N/A literally; flagged host analogue — no kernel panic path exists, but SEC01 rejects valid docs and can stall a future gate.

**S21. No roll-your-own primitives. If a primitive is introduced, it is a well-known algorithm from a reviewed crate (or, if truly novel, it comes with a separate ADR and a cited proof or analysis).**

N/A — no primitive is introduced; review SHA hashes are evidence identity checks, not a new security protocol.

**S22. Keys are never logged, returned in error messages, or exposed via `Debug`.**

N/A — product handles no keys; host audit/log review found no credential/private-content read or dump.

**S23. Constant-time comparisons are used where timing leaks matter.**

N/A — there is no timing-sensitive secret comparison; public path/slug equality is not a key comparison.

**S24. Randomness comes from an acceptable source — not `rand::thread_rng` with no seeding story.**

N/A — no security randomness is used; temporary fixture names are not a cryptographic guarantee.

**S25. Nonces, IVs, and salts are handled correctly per the primitive's contract.**

N/A — no nonce, IV or salt lifecycle is introduced.

**S26. Secrets (keys, tokens, capability bits) are not included in logs, panic messages, debug output, or error types.**

OK for host analogue — no secret/capability logging or private file read in inspected source and audited controls; public diagnostic strings remain public input.

**S27. `Debug` impls on security-sensitive types redact sensitive fields (or are not implemented at all).**

N/A — no Rust Debug or security-sensitive secret type is introduced; host reports use explicit public diagnostic fields.

**S28. Any new dependency has been evaluated under [infrastructure.md](../../../standards/infrastructure.md) dependency-addition rules.**

OK — no new third-party dependency; Python stdlib choice is already accepted by ADR-0043.

**S29. The dependency's trust model is understood. A build-time-only crate is very different from a kernel-linked crate.**

OK — existing host interpreter/stdlib trust is explicit; this code is not kernel-linked and performs no dependency fetch.

**S30. `cargo-vet` trust decisions are updated accordingly.**

N/A — no Cargo dependency/trust change; Cargo.lock unchanged and no cargo-vet execution is claimed.

**S31. The change is reconciled with the documented threat model in [`docs/architecture/security-model.md`](../../../architecture/security-model.md).**

Flagged host analogue — security model read and scope reconciled; SEC01 violates the future gate integrity/availability expectation without changing kernel trust boundaries.

**S32. If the change reshapes the threat model, the PR includes the threat-model update or links to a follow-up PR scheduled imminently.**

N/A for model expansion — no kernel security contract changes; the concrete host parser defect is tracked here for repair, not hidden behind a speculative future update.

### Separate master-plan prompt M33

**M33. Every allocation path returns a typed error, not a panic, on exhaustion.**

**N/A to kernel allocation; bounded host limitation explicitly retained.** This Python CLI has no kernel allocation path and does not claim to catch every `MemoryError`. The 16 MiB source limit, nesting guards, nonregular-file rejection and bounded own resource probes limit supported inputs; no OS out-of-memory fault injection or guaranteed typed exhaustion response was performed. That limitation is not invented as a new blocker. The confirmed host availability blocker is SEC01, independently demonstrated above.

### Architectural principles P1–P12

| Principle | Individual security disposition |
|---|---|
| P1 — No ambient authority | OK host analogue: explicit/default repository root, no product network/process activity and excluded/outside/symlink read guards; the invoker retains normal host authority. |
| P2 — Smallest defensible trusted computing base | OK scope: stdlib host gate only, no kernel TCB addition; source complexity/maintainability remain independent CODE concerns, while SEC01 is concrete here. |
| P3 — Drivers, filesystems, and network stacks live in userspace | N/A runtime architecture: no driver/server placement change; ordinary host pathlib reads add no kernel service. |
| P4 — Capability checks at every trust boundary | Flagged host analogue: filesystem authority checks pass own cases, but source-to-anchor validation admits phantom anchor SEC01; no kernel capability rule changes. |
| P5 — Rust-only for kernel code | OK: Python is host tooling explicitly selected by ADR-0043; no non-Rust kernel code. |
| P6 — HAL separation | N/A: no architecture/BSP/HAL code changes. |
| P7 — No proprietary binary blobs in the kernel | OK: no new kernel blob/dependency; retained evidence snapshots are inert textual script/source records. |
| P8 — Decisions are recorded as ADRs | OK scope: Accepted ADR-0043 and ADR-0040 govern execution; no new policy decision or exception invented. |
| P9 — English in the repository, Turkish in chat | OK: reviewed source/docs/evidence and this report are English. |
| P10 — Mermaid for diagrams | N/A: no new diagram is needed or introduced by this correction/review. |

### New confirmed finding R2-RESUME-SEC02 — quadratic unmatched mixed-delimiter searches

**Medium — host-gate availability; introduced by the current shared-emphasis correction.** An untrusted public document can force repeated full scans of incompatible delimiter openers. This is a concrete measured CPU amplification regression within the documented input size, not a hypothetical universal-resource concern.

Exact fixture generation, with one public `README.md` and no other fixture files:

```python
source = '# ' + '*a ' * 32000 + 'b_ ' * 32000 + '\n'
```

This is **192003 UTF-8 bytes / one physical line**, SHA `98259a9566def8dfa0bae68331003a07fca344e3f8ddb169f7b93f3c948776ac`, well below the16MiB source limit. It contains32,000 opener-only star runs followed by32,000 closer-only underscore runs. Neither family has a matching partner. The expected normal report is `{"checks":["links"],"ok":true,"files":1,"local_targets":0,"fragments":0,"findings":[]}`, exit0, empty stderr.

On exact parent7b44 scripts it completes in **0.290s on Python3.9.6 and0.188s on Python3.13.14**, returning that complete report. On exact current8f7505a scripts, **both processes exceed the10-second deadline** and are killed by the review harness, with no stdout/stderr. A timeout is not an observed product exit status or a valid success/failure JSON report; the recorded exit is null. The timings are local wall-clock observations, not universal host benchmarks.

The independent20-command scaling series also includes1000/2000 repetitions on both revisions/interpreters. Four separate32,000-case confirmations establish the parent/current contrast. Successful resource observations return the exact empty-findings report; no false semantic finding is used as a resource oracle.

Source cause: [plain_emphasis](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L918), especially the reverse stack loop at lines954–960, scans every pending opener for each closer and skips mismatched marker types without recording that they cannot match this closer. For this input there are n star openers and n underscore closers, so the reverse searches perform n×n marker checks. The prior underscore-only stack did not accumulate these star openers. The source explanation and approximately fourfold growth when input doubles corroborate the observed regression. The documented16MiB/64-nesting bounds do not constrain the number of these flat runs.

**Required repair:** retain correct shared star/underscore precedence while avoiding repeated failed searches through the same opener prefix. Marker/closer-class indexed candidate state or appropriately maintained failed-search lower bounds can accomplish this; invalidation and the modulo-three rule still need correct handling. The [CommonMark parsing strategy](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#an-algorithm-for-parsing-nested-emphasis-and-links) describes per-closer-class lower bounds as a relevant primary reference, not a mandate to implement a full renderer. Add meaningful tests-first unmatched mixed-run scaling/termination controls plus matching/nested/crossing/protected-token controls. Re-run the semantic matrices and current resource fixture on an immutable repaired source. Simply accepting the regression because the small semantic examples pass, suppressing the input or claiming a tighter undocumented resource contract would not close SEC02.

No network request, unauthorized file read, memory corruption, kernel denial of service or arbitrary code execution is alleged. The affected availability is the host checker/future required validation gate.
