## 1. Capability correctness — current fix

**OK host authority analogue; kernel capability mechanisms N/A.** The fix adds lexical token records and shared delimiter state, not authority. Explicit/default roots, private/outside/symlink/case/regular-file guards still precede relevant reads; fresh canaries pass. Autolinks contribute protected text without fetch authority. No transfer, duplication, revocation or OS sandbox claim is introduced.

## 2. Trust boundaries — current fix

**Flagged for SEC02 host resource handling; prior semantic findings repaired on current evidence.** Original token provenance now prevents the disclosed source-to-anchor false greens/reds. However untrusted flat mixed-delimiter input can amplify CPU work quadratically inside the host gate. Filesystem boundaries and malformed/read errors pass the stated controls; no userspace/kernel boundary changes.

## 3. Memory safety — current fix

**N/A native/kernel mechanisms; no new host memory-safety finding.** Named token records, Python collections and offset guards add no unsafe/FFI/raw-pointer surface. Complete source review and malformed/nesting/size tests remain relevant host evidence, not CPython memory-safety proof. SEC02 is CPU complexity, not fabricated memory corruption.

## 4. Kernel-mode discipline — current fix

**Flagged host availability analogue, SEC02; literal ISR/scheduler/kernel operation N/A.** The shared search is finite but demonstrably quadratic for a supported-size flat input. Existing suite/setext/backtick controls still pass, but do not cover the failing mixed delimiter family. Fix the concrete search amplification. The old no-universal-resource/OOM/TOCTOU limitations remain.

## 5. Cryptography — current fix

**N/A.** No cryptographic primitive, key handling, randomness, nonce or secret comparison changed. SHA hashes bind review bytes only.

## 6. Secrets and logging — current fix

**OK bounded host evidence.** No new credential/private read, secret diagnostic or environment dump. Fresh audit checks cover autolink and path inputs; public source/error strings remain public diagnostics, not a secret-redaction service.

## 7. Dependencies — current fix

**OK.** Python3.9+ standard library only; Cargo.lock/CI/protection unchanged and no package install. Actual two-interpreter behavior is verified. No cargo-audit/cargo-vet enforcement or execution is invented.

Percent encoding and non-ASCII characters are distinct from that raw character. This is a concrete selected-boundary regression, not a demand for full renderer equivalence.

### Actual reads and repair reasoning

Full current tests1–1426 were personally read FIRST, and both149-method suites were actually run before current implementation/diff reads. I then read the entire parent-to-current six-path source/test/material diff, the complete61-line distinct stage-source diff, current `plain_emphasis` and `inline_text`, all five shared AUTOLINK consumers (label, comment, HTML-context, direct inline text and document inline parsing), heading token/projection consumers and the guide contract140–197. The81-entry read ledger binds previous actual governing/material/source coverage:75 entries unchanged byte-for-byte, six changed entries covered by prior personal full source/body reads plus complete current deltas; tests are freshly read in full. This is not a claim of a redundant fresh full1730-line module or every unchanged document paragraph reread. The current CLI41 lines is unchanged from the previous actual full read.

The [URI class](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L566) now excludes raw U+007F without changing percent-encoded or non-ASCII forms. All consumers share that rule, so an invalid angle candidate falls back to ordinary link/emphasis processing. Current real/phantom ATX/setext controls and embedded missing-file controls preserve exact physical lines and complete reports. This closes CODE C02's direct false-green route; no secret/network/root authority is granted by recognized external autolinks. Primary [CommonMark ASCII-control/autolink definitions](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#autolinks), personally checked in the preceding reconciliation, still govern this unchanged interpretation.

The [emphasis search](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L918) adds a local `bottoms` dictionary keyed by marker, closer opening ability and original run length modulo3. A failed search records a monotonic original projected position, not a mutable stack index. Existing openers below that bound cannot become eligible for that class: marker, original modulo and flanking properties remain unchanged when their remaining length decreases. Intervening removal cannot resurrect an older opener; newly appended openers have later positions and remain searchable. Partial consumption uses the original run length for the class/multiple-of-three rule and remaining counts for actual removal. The stack remains shared across competing markers, preserving the repaired precedence. State is local to each projection/label call, so cache bounds do not cross tokens/documents. The [primary parsing appendix](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#an-algorithm-for-parsing-nested-emphasis-and-links), already personally checked at8733–8749, supports the class-specific failed-search approach. These are source-backed local reasoning and finite controls, not a universal whole-parser complexity or interpreter-soundness proof.
